## The Backlog Directive — restated in full

> **This file is the list of outstanding activities for Phase 6 (Verification & Documentation).** The *how-we-work* rules live in **[CONTRIBUTING.md](../../CONTRIBUTING.md)**; closed/shipped work is archived in **[plan-archive.md](plan-archive.md)**. Split out of the former `plan.md` on 2026-07-09.
>
> **⚠️ Before starting ANY row — Existing-Work-First (CONTRIBUTING.md § Existing-Work-First Policy):** `git status` + `git log` first (uncommitted/fresh work matching the row = it's done or in progress — finish it, don't redo it); check for a second live session (`ps aux | grep claude`) before editing shared files; grep to *disprove* the row's premise (rows go stale — four rows have closed as ALREADY SHIPPED). A §RESUME entry says where the last session *stopped*, not what the tree looks like *now*.
>
> **Legend:** `[ ]` open · `[~]` in-progress / recurring · `[x]` closed (kept here for context; migrate to plan-archive.md over time).

> **⚠️ This file is one of six.** A row's neighbours may live in a sibling phase file, and the Existing-Work-First grep MUST cover all six — `grep -n '§DX-02xx' BACKLOG*.md` — before you conclude a row is unclaimed. The §RESUME entry that closed a row may sit in a different phase file than the row itself.

## Standing Directive — Code Comments (user directive 2026-08-23)

> **Binding on every increment in this file.** Where this and a §-row's own instructions disagree, this wins.

**The failure this prevents:** writing a comment that narrates the change being made right now. That context is real, but it expires the instant the change merges — the defect it describes no longer exists, so the comment becomes a story about a problem no future reader can observe. It is a changelog entry in the wrong file, and a third copy of text already required in the commit body and the PR description.

- **CC-1 (MUST NOT)** Write a comment describing a change, a fix, a defect, its cause, or what the code used to do. No *"was / now / previously / instead of"*, no *"this fixes"*, no *"needed because otherwise"*, no *"note that we no longer"*.
- **CC-2 (MUST)** Apply the **survival test** to every comment before writing it: *would this still be true and useful to someone reading this file a year from now, who never saw the diff?* If it only makes sense beside the diff, it is changelog — delete it and put it in the commit body.
- **CC-3 (MUST)** Default to **zero** comments. Declarative config — Terraform, DNS records, k8s manifests, CI YAML, Helm values — is self-describing and takes none. A resource named `dmarc-example-com` does not need a comment saying it is the DMARC record.
- **CC-4 (MAY)** Comment only when a future editor would actively break something without it: a non-obvious external constraint, a required out-of-band manual step, an invariant the surrounding code cannot show. **One line.** If it needs a paragraph it belongs in a design doc, not inline.
- **CC-5 (MUST)** Before every commit, re-read the comment lines added: `git diff --cached | grep '^+' | grep -E '#|//|/\*'`. Each hit must pass CC-2 on its own. **Deleting is always an acceptable outcome.** *"I already wrote it"*, *"it is only one line"*, and *"this one is genuinely useful"* are not exemptions — the last one is the exact thought that precedes every violation.
- **CC-6 (MUST)** Applies to comments **edited** as well as added. When a change invalidates an existing comment, the default action is **DELETE**, not rewrite it into a new narrative.

> **⚠️ KNOWN TENSION, flagged not resolved — needs a user call.** This repo currently runs an *opposite* convention in `play.html`: **§AUDIT-03n / §DX-02c "annotate, don't rewrite"**, which deliberately preserves defect history inline (e.g. `const NODE_NPC_KEYS`'s 8-line comment explaining that 21 of 26 rows named retired node codes, or `_renderFinalMap`'s §AUDIT-03e note on why the node filter is held at the historical set). Those are exactly the *"what the code used to do"* narratives CC-1 forbids, and several are load-bearing: they are the only record of why a value is held where it is, and deleting them would re-open closed audit rows. **Until the call is made, the working rule is:** CC-1..CC-6 bind all **new** comments; existing §AUDIT-/§DX-annotated blocks are **not** swept under CC-6 — they are a documented archaeology convention, not accidental changelog. Filed as **§DX-02fa** below.

## §BACKLOG — Open Items (Phase 6)

### §DX-02lp — the 460 historical hashes could be mapped to live commits, and the rewrite left a key for part of it (NEW 2026-09-26 during §DX-02lo, 🟡 do it only when someone needs the old commits)

- [ ] **§DX-02lp — option (a) of §DX-02lo, deferred there: annotate each pre-rewrite citation as `` `new` (was `old`) `` and delete its line from `src/scripts/historical-hashes.txt`.** Gate #40 `check:hashes` then ratchets the list down, since a listed hash nothing cites is a finding. **What §DX-02lo learned that makes this cheaper than it looked:** the 2026-08-23 rewrite remapped every hash inside a commit *message* and no file contents. So a pre-rewrite *"record the ship hash X"* commit carries the new hash in its subject and the old one in its diff. Of the 54 such commits, **14** add exactly one dead stamp and are clean old→new pairs, for example `0f5ebe0`: message `72e9d7d`, diff `3d0ab1d`. The rest add up to 86 dead hashes each, because they rewrite whole tables. For the remainder, a `SHIPPED <date> `old`` stamp names its row, and the ship commit is the one whose subject carries that row on that date: `git log --format='%h %ad %s' --date=short | grep '§ROW'`. **Measure first:** how many of the 460 sit in a `SHIPPED` stamp (275 stamps matched in all, dead and live), and how many resolve to exactly one commit by row and date. **Why 🟡:** 313 of the citations are in `plan-archive.md`, a record, so the value is only to someone running `git show` on an old row. **Provenance:** §DX-02lo, 2026-09-26.

### §DX-02ke — the suite's baseline is a property of the host, and the two hosts this repo is worked from disagree by five red tests (NEW 2026-09-14 during §DX-02fo, 🟡 five calls, one per newly-visible red — but establish the common cause first)

- [ ] **§DX-02ke — on a host that can launch Chromium, `npm test --prefix src` reports 7 failures, not the 2 §DX-02js measured.** **Measured 2026-09-14 at `0f40106`, foreground, redirected to a file:** **1229 passed / 7 failed / 4.6m**, and `grep -c 'browserType.launch'` over the log is **0** — §DX-02ir's ENOENT floor is **absent on this host**. §DX-02js measured **152 passed / 1080 failed with 1078 ENOENT** and concluded *"exactly 2 of those 1080 failures are the suite doing its job"*; the 2026-09-13 session recorded a three-shard baseline of **152 passed / 1084 failed**. **Both are correct for the host they ran on.** The 2 were 2 of the tests that *could* run; the other five sit inside files the floor never let execute. **The seven, re-derived at HEAD and proved unrelated to the row that found them** (identical list with the working tree stashed): `dx02ee-scalar-field-clear.test.js:103` — §DX-02ee, `editField('quest', k, 'bits', null)` returns `ok:true` where the test asserts `false` · `quest-runtime-uqf.test.js:2807` (Wave 1v, `lxvii67`'s `activateCond` behind a `_legacyFn` gate) · `:7658` (Wave 2bc) · `:8250` (Wave 3a) · `:8405` (Wave 3b) · `uqf-gate-ast.test.js:17` (§VM-01-F full-corpus differential) · `web-of-connections-l45.spec.js:215` (Layer 45 bracketed editorial placeholder). **FIVE OF THE SEVEN ARE THE UQF/QUEST-VM CORPUS, which suggests one cause and not five — establish that before opening five calls.** **The durable half of this row is not the seven.** It is that **a §RESUME baseline is only true for the host that measured it**, and this repo is worked from at least two: one where Chromium launches and one where it does not. A hand-off that writes *"baseline 152/1084"* without naming the host sends the next session hunting a regression that is a missing browser. **Recommend: record the host with the baseline** — one line naming whether `browserType.launch` succeeded and the passed/failed pair — wherever a §RESUME entry or session-state block states test counts. **Verify:** the seven named individually, each fixed or recorded with the call that keeps it; and a session-state block that states a test baseline also states whether browsers launched. **Relates to §DX-02js** (whose two are two of these seven) and **§DX-02ir** (the floor itself). **Provenance:** §DX-02fo, whose third verification leg came back with a baseline the hand-off did not predict.

### §DX-02jw — `check:legacycodes` cannot see a single retired node code in running prose, and the one instance it missed was found by hand (NEW 2026-09-08 during §DX-02jr, 🟡 the fix is a heuristic change and it has a false-positive budget)

- [ ] **§DX-02jw — `docs/design/world.md:1334` read *"The CY pit training bouts are informal affairs"* for as long as anyone can date, `world.md` is a SWEEP doc, and gate #16 passed the whole time.** **Measured 2026-09-08 at `8d8d8fb`, with the line still present:** `npm run legacy --prefix src` reported **0 still in live docs (SWEEP+PENDING)** and `check:legacycodes` was green in a **27/27** walk. **The line has since been swept** — it is *"The Undercity pit training bouts"* now, corrected as part of §DX-02jr's doc sync, because a doc describing the pit could not go on calling it by the code the game had just stopped using. **So this row has no live red to point at, and that is the row's problem, not an argument against it:** the gap was found by a person reading, which is the failure mode a gate exists to remove. **The cause is `nodeContextLine`, and it is a deliberate design being paid for.** A token counts only inside a *node context*: a `NODE_MAP`/`CELL_GRID` mention, the word *node*, an arrow, an `R07,C16` grid ref, a compass word, a table cell whose whole content is the code — **or two or more codes on the line**. Line 1334 has one code and none of the other tells, so it falls to `codes.length >= 2` and returns false. **The rule exists for a good reason** — the script's own header records it: a blind replace turns `AT`/`IS`/`SE`/`CA` into nonsense inside English sentences — **so this is a coverage gap in a guard that was built to avoid a worse failure, not a bug.** **Measured blind spot: 54 single-code lines across the 11 SWEEP docs that the rule does not reach.** They are not 54 defects: the great majority are `world.md` item-table rows where the code is an NPC's location tag (`Dora Flint (EI)`) and `story-flowchart.md` ASCII art. **The one genuine instance was 1334, and it is the exact shape §DX-02jr was filed about** — a retired code standing in for a place, in prose, in a doc a reader takes as current. **The other 53 have not been read hit-by-hit, so the count of real ones among them is unknown and is the row's second measurement.** **Fix — the call is where the new signal comes from.** (a) **A prose test**: a line with sentence punctuation and no table pipe or box-drawing character is prose, and a single code in prose counts. Cheap, and the 54 above are mostly excluded by the pipe alone. (b) **An adjacency test**: a code immediately followed by a common noun (`pit`, `node`, `district`, `checkpoint`) is a place reference. Narrower, more precise, needs a word list nobody maintains. (c) **Leave the gate and fix 1334**, recording that single-code prose is out of scope. **Recommend (a), with the 54 published in the report** so the false-positive budget is visible before the gate is armed — the §DX-02jd shape, where what the check *could not* see is reported rather than implied. **Verify — and note the shape, because the obvious verification is no longer available:** `world.md:1334` is already swept, so the gate cannot be shown red against it at HEAD. The proof is a **planted negative control** — restore the original sentence in a scratch copy, and the gate must name it — plus the 53 remaining single-code lines read hit-by-hit and classified, and a report line stating how many single-code lines the new rule counts and how many it still skips. **Provenance:** §DX-02jr, which swept `CY` out of the game, then found the doc describing the same pit still calling it `CY` with a green gate over it, and swept that too — leaving the gap without its exhibit.

### §DX-02js — two browser-free tests are red in the committed tree, and the suite that would have said so is read as unavailable (NEW 2026-09-08 during §DX-02jq, 🟡 two calls: is the assertion stale or the engine wrong, once each)

- [ ] **§DX-02js — `npm test --prefix src` runs here, reports 152 passed and 1080 failed, and exactly 2 of those 1080 failures are the suite doing its job.** **Measured 2026-09-08 at `585b9c7`:** the suite collects **1232 tests in 127 files** and takes **12.6m**. Parsing the failure report into blocks gives **1080**, of which **1078** carry `browserType.launch: Failed to launch: Error: spawn /home/user/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium_headless_shell-1223/chrome-linux/headless_shell ENOENT` — §DX-02ir, expected, and not this row. **The remaining 2 are assertion failures in tests that never open a page**, and both are red at HEAD independently of the change that found them (stashed, the two files alone: **14 passed, 2 failed**):
> **(a) `tests/integration/dx02ee-scalar-field-clear.test.js:103`** — §DX-02ee *"a structured field is left to editStructuredField, not half-removed"*. `W.editField('quest', key, 'bits', null)` is expected to be **refused** (`r.ok` → `false`) and returns `true`. The test's next line round-trips the quest and expects it unchanged, so the assertion is guarding the write path §DX-02ee shipped: `=null` clears an unquoted scalar and must not touch a structured field. **Either `editField` lost the structured-field guard, or §DX-02ee's contract changed under it and the test was not told.**
> **(b) `tests/integration/dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js:130`** — §DX-02l *"sweep deletes one the chain already holds, and `--force` discards the rest"*. The assertion is `existsSync(… \`${path.basename(ARCHIVED_NAME,'.html')}.patch\`)` → expected `true`, received `false`: the sweep did not leave the patch the chain is supposed to hold. This is the snapshot chain, which `play-20260907-114604.html` in the repo root suggests is being exercised by hand.
> **Why nobody has seen these, and it is not only §DX-02jd's reason.** §DX-02jd is right that the browser-free third of the suite runs and reports and nobody looks. **But the immediate cause here is narrower and cheaper to fix: the suite's collection depends on the working directory, and one of the two answers is silent.** `npx playwright test --list` from the **repo root** prints `Error: No tests found` / **0 tests in 0 files**; from `src/` it prints **1232 tests in 127 files**. The config is `src/playwright.config.js` and `npm test --prefix src` finds it. **§DX-02fg's close recorded the root number as evidence** — *"`npm test` not run, reason measured not inherited: `playwright test --list` collects 0 tests in 0 files here, which is §DX-02ir"* — and it is not §DX-02ir, it is a `cd`. A session that measures honestly and measures from the wrong directory writes down a false negative and cites the right row for it.
> **Fix — three parts, and only the first two are this row's.** (1) **Decide (a) and (b)**: one call each, engine-vs-assertion, with the evidence in the row. (2) **Make the false negative impossible to record**: the invocation `AGENTS.md` documents is already `npm test --prefix src`, so the cheap half is a line in the verify block saying that a bare `npx playwright test` at the root collects nothing and is not evidence of anything. (3) **The wrapper that judges the run rather than the selection is §DX-02jd's**, unchanged and still recommended there — this row is the second time its absence has cost something, and the first time the cost was measured in red assertions rather than argued.
> **Verify:** with the two decisions made, `npm test --prefix src` at HEAD reports **0** non-`browserType.launch` failure blocks, by the same block parse; and the count of browser-launch failures is stated rather than implied, so a future run can tell a new red test from the musl floor. **Provenance:** §DX-02jq, on running the third verification leg because the reason recorded for skipping it did not reproduce.


### §DX-02jd — a third of the test suite never opens a browser, and the host that cannot open one runs none of it (NEW 2026-09-07 during §GR-FU2, 🟡 one design call: how the browser-free subset is selected)

- [ ] **§DX-02jd — §DX-02ir says the third verification leg cannot run on this host. A third of it can, and does, and nobody looks.** Playwright launches a browser only for a test that takes the `page` fixture, so on the musl box the browser-free tests run and report normally while the rest fail `spawn … ENOENT`. **Measured at `ba29134`:** `grep -rhoE '^\s*test(\.[a-z]+)?\(' src/tests | wc -l` = **1502** test declarations, **1001** of them naming `page` in their fixture object and **427** plain `() =>` bodies. Run alone on this host, `tests/integration/audit03n-npc-registries.test.js` reports **4 passed, 2 failed**, and both failures are its two `{ page }` tests.
> **The cost of not having it was paid the same day this was filed.** §GR-FU added `kenickie` to `SWEELINCK_NAMING_LINES` and left `audit03n-npc-registries.test.js`'s *"exactly the six canonical profile keys"* assertion **red in the committed tree** — a pure-Node assertion that runs fine here, missed because `npm test` fails as a whole and a session reads the whole run as unavailable. §GR-FU2 found it only because it touched the same table, and repaired it there.
> **Fix — the call is how the subset is selected.** (a) **Tag them** — `@nobrowser` in each title, `--grep @nobrowser`; exact, and 427 edits that rot the moment a test grows a `page`. (b) **A second Playwright project** — cleaner, except that files mix the two kinds, so `testMatch` cannot express it. (c) **Judge the run, not the selection** — a wrapper that runs the suite and fails only on a failure that is *not* a browser-launch error, reporting how many tests the missing browser took with it. **Recommend (c):** it edits no test, it degrades to the full verdict unchanged on a glibc host, and it is the only one of the three that reports what it could not check.
> **This is §DX-02ir option (a) made concrete and cheap** — no root, no distro chromium, no `executablePath` env var — and it does not close §DX-02ir, which is still the right row for making the browser half runnable.
> **Provenance:** §GR-FU2, on running the two tests that touch the registry it changed.
> **Note 2026-09-28 (§DX-02ir `c057410`):** the musl host now launches a distro chromium, so the whole suite runs there. This row now matters only for a host with neither browser.



### §DX-02jb — the gate number every doc cites is a chain position maintained by hand, and one insert shifted five of them silently (NEW 2026-09-07 during §DX-02iw, 🟡 assert it or stop numbering)

- [ ] **§DX-02jb — `docs/design/index.md` names each gate `` `check:walk` gate #N ``, N is its index in `GATES`, and nothing anywhere checks that.** 🟡 **the call is whether the number is a fact or a label.** §DX-02iv inserted `check:arraypatch` at chain position 18 on 2026-09-06 and renumbered nothing, so `check-spdx` (#18), `check-backlogcounts` (#19), `check-condition-prices` (#20) and `check-docpointers` (#21) each cited their left-hand neighbour's position until §DX-02iw renumbered them 2026-09-07 — **four rows wrong for a day, and no gate could say so.** **The drift is not hypothetical anywhere else either:** `check:dupkeys` is cited as **gate #11** in `lab-report-npc-card-map.md:304`, `lab-report-highway-mesh-entry.md:217` and `lab-report-node-network-reconnection.md:210`, and has sat at chain position **2** since the chain was reordered — a number that was true when written and has been false ever since. **Two options.** (a) **Assert it:** a phase that parses `` gate #(\d+) `` out of `docs/design/index.md`, resolves the script named in the same row back to its `GATES` index, and fails on a mismatch — `check:backlogcounts` is the model, a count in a file asserted against the thing it counts, and this one has the same shape and the same failure mode in both directions. (b) **Stop numbering:** cite gates by script or script name only, and delete the `#N` from `index.md`. **Recommend (a), and leave the lab reports alone** — they are dated records of what was true when measured, and §AUDIT-03n's *annotate, don't rewrite* covers them; it is the maintained index that has to be right today. **Verify:** plant a reordered `GATES` and see the new phase go red. **Provenance:** §DX-02iw, whose own renumbering of `index.md` is the repair this row would have made unnecessary. **More evidence, 2026-09-25 (§DOCPTR-02):** `check-docpointers` sits at position **23** in `check:walk:serial`, `index.md` calls it **#22**, and §DOCPTR-02's own row called it **#21**. `ce687ce`'s subject copied the row's #21, so a stale number spreads into `git log` too.
> **DECIDED 2026-09-26 by the user — now 🟢.** **(a) Assert it** with a phase that fails when an `index.md` `gate #N` disagrees with `GATES`; the lab reports stay as dated records.

### §DX-02hy — the failure path still calls `process.exit(1)`, and that is the same deadlock (NEW 2026-08-25 during §DX-02fz, 🟡 one design call: how a script signals failure without exiting)

- [ ] **§DX-02hy — §DX-02fz removed `process.exit(0)` from 18 scripts and left every `process.exit(1)` in place.** **The mechanism does not care about the code:** the sampled deadlock is `node::Environment::Exit → DisposePlatform → NodePlatform::Shutdown → WorkerThreadsTaskRunner::Shutdown → uv_thread_join → __ulock_wait`, reached from `process.exit()` with any argument. Measured at `PENDING`: **17 of the last 18 trials hung** with `process.exit(0)` present and **0 of 40** without, on `check-mover-behaviour.js`. Nothing about that measurement is specific to the zero.
> **Why it was scoped out rather than swept:** every recorded §DX-02fz reproduction is a **green** gate hanging after printing its ✓, because that is the path a passing session takes hundreds of times. A failing gate hangs a session that was already going to stop — worse to read, far rarer to hit. Fixing it is also not a deletion: `process.exit(1)` sits **mid-file** and must stop execution *and* set a non-zero code, so each site needs `process.exitCode = 1;` plus a `return` that is correct where it stands.
> **The design call:** (a) **`process.exitCode = 1; return;` at each site**, top-level `return` being legal in CommonJS — mechanical, but ~18 sites each needing its control flow read; (b) **throw instead**, letting an uncaught exception set the code, which changes every failure's output shape and would need each gate's message re-checked; (c) **leave it, and rely on `run-gates.js`'s 120 s per-gate deadline**, which already converts a hang into a named failure inside `check:walk` — but not for a gate run directly, which is what every doc tells a session to do when one goes red. **Recommendation: (a)**, and it wants its own increment because the verification is per-site, not per-file.
> **Provenance:** §DX-02fz, which named the mechanism and fixed the half that fires on the common path.

### §DX-02hr — 72% of every test's time is Chromium re-parsing the same 5.3 MB `play.html` (NEW 2026-08-25 during §DX-02hq, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL: is a reused page still an honest test)

- [ ] **§DX-02hr — the suite's remaining cost is not parallelism, it is that each of ~1000 tests loads the whole game from cold.** **Measured at `b0c236d`** with a probe spec (`seedAndLoad` timed against a bare `page.evaluate`): **`seedAndLoad` = 326 ms, a subsequent `evaluate` = 7 ms**, against a typical `quest-runtime-uqf` test of ~450 ms. `play.html` is **5.3 MB** and Playwright's `{ page }` fixture is a fresh context per test, so the cache is cold every time: the suite performs **~649 `page.goto`/`seedAndLoad` calls** and pays a full parse for each. §DX-02hq took the free 24% (7.4m → 5.6m) by running tests inside a file concurrently; **the next 30–40% is here, and it is not free.**
> **Ruled out, so nobody re-measures them:** the static host is **not** the bottleneck (`npx serve` answers `play.html` in **40 ms for one request and 80 ms for 32 concurrent**), and **more workers buy nothing** (333s at the default vs 329s at `workers: '100%'`, and the oversubscribed run flaked a UI click) — both recorded in `playwright.config.js`'s own comments.
> **The design call:** a worker-scoped page that is loaded once and **re-seeded** per test instead of reloaded would skip the parse, but it trades away the property that makes these tests trustworthy — every test currently starts from a genuinely fresh engine, and a leaked global would be invisible. That is the same class of untrustworthy green as §DX-02hb and §DX-02hm. **Options:** (a) opt-in only, for the read-only assertion clusters in `quest-runtime-uqf.test.js` that never mutate `S_story`; (b) a reused page plus a hard reset assertion in `afterEach` that fails if any known global drifted; (c) leave it, and treat 5.6m as the price of a cold start per test.
> **Recommendation: (a).** It is bounded to the file that is 303 of the 1033 tests, the read-only clusters are identifiable (they call `page.evaluate` against `validateQuest`/`adaptLegacyQuest`/`canActivate` without touching state), and it leaves every stateful test on the cold path it has today.
> **Provenance:** §DX-02hq, sizing where the suite's wall time actually goes after the parallelism was fixed.

### §DX-02hj — the `r2h → coc` rename reached the harness and not the wire (NEW 2026-08-25 during §DX-02hi, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL: rename the headers or keep them)

- [ ] **§DX-02hj — three response headers still carry the retired product name, and the test that would have said so asserted the new one.** **Measured at `779f5c0`:** `/api/world/download` emits `X-R2H-ServerId`, `X-R2H-WorldHash`, `X-R2H-EngineVer` (`wbapi-server.js@6429`), and `wbapi-server.js:1442`'s `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` names the same three. **The reader agrees** — `edit.html:10646-10647` reads `X-R2H-ServerId` / `X-R2H-WorldHash`. So the shipped contract is self-consistent and the **only** thing naming `x-coc-*` was `mud-harness.mjs`, whose assertion `a0801b7` updated while leaving the code alone. That assertion has been red ever since, in a job nothing was reading.
> **Why it is a call and not a 🟢:** these headers are wire format. Renaming them is a coordinated change across the server, `edit.html`, and any peer server already running — mesh peers are other people's deployments, and the download endpoint is how a world crosses between them. The cheap alternative is to keep `X-R2H-*` as the wire name and note in `spec-engine.md` that the prefix predates the `coc` rename.
> **Recommendation:** emit **both** for one release — new name authoritative, old name alongside — then drop `X-R2H-*`. That is the only version of the rename that does not break a peer mid-flight.
> **Provenance:** the harness's third failing assertion in §DX-02hi. Pointed at the shipped header there so the job could go green; this row is the actual decision.

### §DX-02gf — one mesh test is green alone and red in the suite, and it has been red in every full run measured (NEW 2026-08-24 during §DX-02gd, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL: fix the wait or fence the test)

- [ ] **§DX-02gf — `multiplayer-presence.test.js:174` fails only under full-suite load.** **Measured 2026-08-24, two full `npm test` runs at different trees:** *"remote players move on the watcher's minimap in real time (§MESH-01-FU 4)"* failed **both times, including the retry**, at `expect(await w.page.evaluate(() => [...document.querySelectorAll('#mini-map-grid .mmc')].some((cell) => cell.textContent === '☺'))).toBe(true)` — the watcher's grid never shows the mover's dot. The **same file run alone passes 7/7**, also twice. Nothing in either tree touched mesh, presence, or the minimap: the first run was at `41e72d1` + an item-key rename, the second at the same plus a doc-anchor refresh. Full-suite totals were **979/981** and **980/981**; this case is the only red common to both.
> **The shape says timing, not logic.** The assertion reads a DOM state produced by a second real browser context over SSE, with no `expect.poll`/`waitFor` around it — a fixed `await` and then a single synchronous read. Under a saturated worker the mover's `to:{r,c}` broadcast has simply not been rendered by the watcher yet. The neighbouring cases in the same file (`§MESH-01-FU 3`, `5`) do wait on a condition, and they stay green in both modes.
> **THE CALL:** (a) **poll the assertion** — wrap it in `expect.poll(...)` / `toPass()` with a bounded timeout, so the test asserts *eventually shows the dot* rather than *shows the dot at this instant*; smallest, and it is what the sibling cases already do; (b) **serialise the mesh file** — `test.describe.configure({ mode: 'serial' })` plus a worker fence, which hides the race instead of stating the contract; (c) **leave it and mark it flaky**, which makes every future full-suite run report a red the operator must remember to discount. **(a) recommended.** **Verify:** ten consecutive `npm test` runs with no red in this file; the isolated run stays 7/7.
> **Why it matters beyond one case.** `resume.md` §4 step 7 tells every session to run `npm test` and read the summary counts. A suite that is reliably 980/981 trains the reader to accept a red line, which is the state in which a real regression arrives unnoticed. **This is the §DX-02fz lesson in the test suite** — a green that is not trustworthy is worse than a red.
> ⚠️ **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY, BY THIS ROW'S OWN AUTHOR (during §DX-02aj).** The headline above said *red in every full run measured* on a sample of two. **The third full run passed it.** So the case is **intermittent at roughly 2-in-3, not deterministic** — which does not weaken the row, it sharpens it: an intermittent red is worse than a reliable one, because the operator cannot tell a flake from a regression by looking, and a green run proves nothing about the next. **Option (c), "leave it and mark it flaky", is now the option to reject hardest.** The recommendation stands at (a), and the verification bar rises with the correction: **ten consecutive full runs**, not one green, because a 2-in-3 failure has a 1-in-3 chance of looking fixed on any single pass.
> **Provenance:** §DX-02gd, found in the two full-suite runs that closed that row; corrected during §DX-02aj on the third.

That one string is the only thing blocking the row.

Over GitHub's 50 MB **warning** line, well under the 100 MB hard block, so the push succeeds with a warning. It is a revision of the one file the author asked to preserve completely, so removing it means accepting a hole in that history. **Decision deferred deliberately.**
- [ ] **701 bare `CodexOfConquest` mentions remain in HISTORY docs** (`docs/lab-reports/`, `docs/archive/`, `docs/backlog/plan-archive.md`). Left as written under annotate-don't-rewrite (§DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m); README's *A note on names* explains it to anyone who greps. **Open question:** is that the right call for a public repo, where the first reader has no context? 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL.
- [ ] **`make run` has never been executed end-to-end.** `make status`, `./bin/api help`, the server launcher and `run.sh` syntax are verified, but the full `run` target — two Terminal windows plus a browser open — has not been run in this environment. **Verify on a real desktop session before relying on it.**

### §DX-02fx — the anchor-convention suite repairs the docs it is auditing, so its own verdict depends on run order (NEW 2026-08-23 during §DX-01g, 🟡 ONE DESIGN CALL: assert or repair, not both)

- [ ] **`src/tests/integration/dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` has a write side-effect on tracked files.** Its third test runs the real gate with `--fix` against the repo root — `expect(run(['--fix']).code).toBe(0)` at `src/tests/integration/dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js:115` — and `--fix` rewrites stale `symbol@NNNN` hints **in place, in the working tree**. Running the suite is therefore a mutation of tracked documentation, not a read-only check.
- **The consequence is an order-dependent verdict.** The second test (`:71`) asserts every game-file anchor in `MIGRATED` is **line-exact**, and it runs *before* the `--fix` test. At `HEAD` (`c06d4f7`) `BACKLOG.md` carried **121 stale hints**, so that assertion was **red on a clean checkout** and went green only on the *next* run, after the earlier run's `--fix` had repaired the docs. A suite that green-lights itself on the second pass is reporting the state it created, not the state it found.
- **Evidence, this session.** First run: `1 failed` on `:71`, the other 4 green. No source change between runs — only the `--fix` write. Second run: `5 passed`.
- **The design call.** Either (a) the `--fix` test operates on a **temp copy** so the suite is read-only and `:71` tells the truth about a clean checkout, or (b) `:71` drops to the gate's own standard (*resolves*, not *line-exact*) and staleness stays a warning, as `check:anchors` already treats it. **(a) is the one that keeps a real assertion.** What must not stay is both.
- **Grep to disprove:** `grep -n "\-\-fix" src/tests/integration/dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js`.
> ⚠️ **REPRODUCED AGAIN 2026-08-24 (during §DX-02gd), and the blast radius is now measured.** One `npm test` rewrote **2,093 stale hint lines across 118 tracked documents** in a single run — every backlog phase file, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `plan-archive.md`, `docs/design/*` and 100+ lab reports — and also regenerated the tracked binary `src/test-results/l44-final-map.png`, which no assertion in the suite reads. The order-dependence held exactly as described: `:71` **red on run 1** (a one-line insert into `play.html` had shifted the `MIGRATED` docs' hints), **green on run 2** with no source change between them. **A row-closing session cannot tell its own doc edits from the suite's** without `git diff`-ing before and after, which is how this increment found it. The PNG is the cheaper half of option (a): nothing needs it written at all.
