### §AUDIT-03af — the engine's own comment is wrong about the past, and the wrong diagnosis produced the wrong fix (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02u, 🟡 mixed)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03af — `§VM-01-G1-FIX: originally keyed to 'LJ3', a dead@31576` states two things about history and both are false.** The comment reads *"originally keyed to `'LJ3'`, a dead node code **no NODE_MAP entry ever carried**, so this panel had **NEVER rendered**; remapped to LSO … the quest's own `activateNode`."* At §SIREN-01's birth commit `42c2f82` (2026-05-28) the file carries `LJ3:{ num:117, code:'LJ3', name:'junction', label:'The Serpent Passage', act:4 …}` — a real `NODE_MAP` entry carrying the arc's hardest battle (`battle:{label:'The Serpent of the Passage', key:'sea_serpent', count:1}`). **The panel therefore DID render, for 13 days, exactly where designed.** `LJ3` was **RETIRED** on 2026-06-10 by `a61d6eb`, not born dead.
> **The correction is CAUSAL, not cosmetic.** `{ id:'story-lso-trigger', nodes:['LSO'],@31580` is a *teaser*: it exists to tell the player there is something in the water **to the east** and send them to find it. Believing it had never worked, the fix pointed it at **`LSO` — the fog bank itself** — so it now announces the discovery to a player standing inside it: *"They are also, in a second and quieter register, speaking to something in the water to the east."*
> **This is §DOC-02g's rule in live form, 2nd confirmed instance: a migration commit's own comment is a CLAIM ABOUT THE PAST, usually written from memory rather than from the diff. Being "in the code" confers no authority about history — verify it with `git show <sha>` like any other claim.** The generalisable shape: ***a dead node code is usually a RETIREMENT, and diagnosing it as born-dead licenses a remap that a retirement would forbid.***
> **Fix, two halves.** (a) 🟢 **Correct the comment** — one line, no behaviour change, and worth doing alone so the next reader is not misled again. (b) 🟡 **Re-place the panel** — small design call, and it is downstream of **§SIREN-01-FU**: with LJ0–LJ3 gone there is no "crossing before the fog", so either the teaser moves to whatever node ends up preceding LSO, or it folds into LSO's own node text and stops being a teaser. **Do not do (b) before §SIREN-01-FU decides the geography.** *(Worth a sweep: this is the only §VM-01-G1 dead-panel remap whose premise §DOC-02u checked — the other remaps in that pass, §PLAY-01-G / §NPC-01-SF4, cite the same "born dead" standard and were not re-verified.)*
> **(c) THE SWEEP IS DONE — §DOC-02y, 2026-08-12, and the verdict is the OPPOSITE of `LJ3`'s: fix the comments, keep the remaps.** `const birkaNpcs@35347` justifies the §PLAY-01-G remap and closes *"**No NODE_MAP entry ever existed for CI/IN/TV/BA/CY**, so these cards previously rendered nowhere"*; two lines later §NPC-01-SF4 adds *"No NODE_MAP entry ever existed for CQ/SQ/GC either."* **Eight claims; at `32c10c5` (2026-05-24, earliest surviving build) SEVEN ARE FALSE** — `CI:{num:1,'city',"City Streets — Birka"}` · `IN:{num:2,'inn','The First Inn'}` · `TV:{num:3,'tavern','Birka Tavern'}` · `BA:{num:4,'bar','The Rough Bar'}` · `CY:{num:6,'cyberpunk_streets','Neon Undercity'}` · `SQ:{num:35,'scholars_qtr',"Scholar's Quarter — Weimar"}` · `GC:{num:26,'goblin_cave','Goblin Warrens'}` are all real `NODE_MAP` entries. **Only `CQ` holds.** ***And the comment transcribes those very labels — "The First Inn", "Birka Tavern", "The Rough Bar", "Neon Undercity" — character for character off the records the same sentence denies:*** self-refutation inside one comment, §DOC-02q's rule appearing in engine source. **But all seven remaps are CORRECT RENAMES**, verified by a `num` + terrain-key + label triple-match against the archive (`IN`→`TLL` num 2, `TV`→`MHQ` num 3, `BA`→`LLA` num 4, `CY`→`HKG` num 6, `CI`→`LHR` num 1, `SQ`→`NUE` num 35, `GC`→`TRD` num 26) — §DOC-02f's *"the node survived, only the key was renamed"* confirmed across seven pairs. The false premise produced seven right answers because the author was silently matching on labels. **So (c) is 🟢 and mechanical:** replace *"never existed"* with *"retired by §WALK/§NAV-01; the node survived under a new key, `num` preserved"* in both comments. ***The durable form: a migration comment's "never existed" is the least reliable sentence in a codebase — it is the one claim the migrator had no reason to check, because the remap already worked. Its danger is not the sentence, it is the STANDARD it sets for the next remap, which may have no label to match on — which is exactly how `LJ3` went wrong.***

### §DX-02cz — `check:questgraph` calls every `countMin` clause satisfiable because counters are monotone, and never asks whether the counter has a writer (NEW 2026-08-18 during §DOC-02cb, 🟡 one detector extension)

- [ ] **§DX-02cz — the written-by-nothing detector covers flags and stops there.** 🟡 **extend one scan; the resource is already collected.** The gate already parses counters into its resource pool at `src/scripts/check-questgraph.js:out.resources.add('count:' + c.path.split('.')[0])@251`, then its satisfiability pass lists `countMin` among the clauses that are *"monotone-satisfiable (the clock always reaches the window)"* and moves on. For a flag the gate asks **who writes it**; for a counter it asks nothing. That is the exact blind spot §DX-02cy hid in for 85 days, in the arc the gate was written to protect.
> **The check is cheap and the corpus is small.** Three counters are read by `completion.countMin` today — `catKills` (5 quests), `monsterKills` (6) and `frCatKillCount` (1) — and a host-writer scan of the inline script for `S_story.<counter>[` / `S_story.<counter> =` answers all three. **Self-test it by deleting a writer** (instrument 114): with the fix in place, reverting §DX-02cy's rename must move the gate's output, and today it does not move at all.
> **Note the polarity against §DX-02bs.** That row is a *false negative* from a host-writer pattern that credits too much (`missionBit:`); this is a false negative from a whole resource class that is never asked. Same detector, opposite causes — fix them in one pass and re-pin the printed counts once.

### §DX-02dn — `mechanics.md` says two drop functions were "retired" and "deleted"; neither has ever existed in the engine (NEW 2026-08-21 during §DOC-02ci, 🟠 two-line prose fix, NO DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02dn — a doc-sync fix corrected the numbers and promoted the wrong diagnosis to fact.** `mechanics.md:231` reads *"The old parallel 15%/12% separate dagger and weapon rolls are retired"* and `mechanics.md:530` reads *"the old `_rollWeaponDrop()` 12%/battle path is **deleted**"*. **Instrument 67 (`git log -S <sym> --all`, NO pathspec, then re-scoped):** `_rollMainWeaponDrop` and `_rollWeaponDrop` appear in exactly seven commits, **every one a documentation commit** (`BACKLOG.md`, `plan-archive.md`, `mechanics.md`, `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`, `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-play-review.md`). Re-run scoped to `*.html`: **zero commits, ever, in any HTML file in the repository history.** The only drop function that has ever shipped is `` `function _rollMonsterWeaponDrop(monsterDmgDie) {@24641` `` (4 HTML commits). **The docs were never describing deleted code — they were describing an API that was never implemented.**
> **PROVENANCE, and it is the point of the row.** The pre-fix doc (`mechanics.md:490`–`:511` at `3a0db52^`) was wrong about the *engine* — *"42 entries"* against a real 70, `minLevel = baseLv + magic × 4` against a real `level ≥ magic × 5`, and two invented drop rates. §PLAY-01-G caught all four and fixed three correctly. But the parent review had concluded *"Both functions are deleted"*, and `ac651a3` wrote that conclusion **into the doc as fact**. A file that carried one unverifiable claim now carries a confident, sourced, wrong one. **This is §AUDIT-03m-FU in its live form: annotation without verification launders a wrong claim into a confident-looking live one** — and the laundering agent was the fix, not the drift.
> **Fix (two lines, no call):** rewrite both to say the rates and functions were **specified but never implemented**, in the §AUDIT-03n/§DX-02c annotate-do-not-erase form, so the history stays greppable — e.g. *"a 15%/12% split roll was specified in the Layer-24 notes and never built; the shipped path is the single guaranteed `_rollMonsterWeaponDrop()`."* **Do NOT write "removed"** — that is the error this row exists to retire. **Verify:** `git log -S_rollWeaponDrop --all -- '*.html'` returns empty, and no doc asserts a deletion.
> **Detector wanted, and it is cheap and general:** a gate that flags any doc sentence asserting **removal** (*deleted · retired · removed · replaced*) of a `symbol()` whose name has **0 commits ever in the code files** — a removal claim is falsifiable by exactly one `git log -S`, and the corpus now has two instances (this row, and §DOC-02c's seven §WALK corridor symbols). Cheaper than `check:deadconsts` because it needs no parse.

### §DX-02fa — the new Code Comments directive contradicts the repo's own annotate-don't-rewrite convention (NEW 2026-08-23, 🟡 POLICY, ONE DESIGN CALL)

- [ ] **§DX-02fa — CC-1 forbids exactly what §AUDIT-03n/§DX-02c requires.** 🟡 The Standing Directive at the top of this file (user directive 2026-08-23) says **CC-1 (MUST NOT): write a comment describing a change, a fix, a defect, its cause, or what the code used to do** — no *"was/now/previously"*, and **CC-6** makes DELETE the default when a change invalidates a comment. `play.html` runs the opposite convention on purpose: **§AUDIT-03n / §DX-02c "annotate, don't rewrite."** Live examples, all load-bearing: `const NODE_NPC_KEYS = {@27547` carries an 8-line note recording that **21 of 26 rows named a node code that does not exist**, why the remap chose the values it did, and why removing the 20 `E*` placeholders is a no-op; `function _renderFinalMap()@27746` carries the §AUDIT-03e note explaining that the node filter is **deliberately held at the historical 129-entry set** and that widening it is an authored call (§AUDIT-03e-FU); `const NPC_FAREWELLS = {@27561` records the one **content override** where the text was treated as the authority over the node code.
> **These are the only record of why those values are what they are.** Delete them under CC-6 and the next agent re-derives a closed audit row from scratch, or "fixes" a deliberate hold. But they are also, literally, *"what the code used to do"* — CC-1's exact prohibition.
> **THE CALL:** (a) **Scope CC-1..CC-6 to NEW comments only** and carve out `§`-tagged archaeology blocks as a named exception in the directive — *recommended*, it keeps both rules honest and is a two-sentence edit; (b) **apply CC uniformly** and migrate the ~dozen §AUDIT/§DX annotation blocks into a `docs/` archaeology file with `→ doc:` pointers back — faithful to CC-3/CC-4, more work, and the pointers are the class §DX-02ey(b) just found unfenced; (c) **exempt `play.html`** entirely and apply CC to `src/js/`, `src/scripts/`, `src/tests/` only. **(a) recommended.** *Interim working rule, already written into the directive: CC binds all new comments; existing §-tagged blocks are not swept.*

### §DX-02cc — the ph3 PATCH gate asserts the branch that had 191 consumers and not the branch that has 3,012 (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bo, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02cc — `check:arraypatch` never exercises a plain-object field, which is the shape the path's own follow-up fix existed for.** 🟢 **two checks, ~15 minutes.** `src/scripts/check-array-patch.js` (13 checks, green at HEAD) was written on 2026-06-26 when the server's PUT dispatch still routed **plain objects** to the in-memory-only `ns.put` — so the gate's object coverage is `killGoals`, an *array of* objects, and nothing else. **§MATH-01 (`32d7bb0`, 2026-07-07) moved bare objects onto `editStructuredField`** after they were found being silently lost on file-watch reload (its own commit message: *"PUT routed plain-object fields (gate/completion) to a memory-only path"*), and **added no assertion**. Measured through `wbapi-core` at HEAD, objects are now the **larger** population: top-level `gate` on **2,823** quests and `completion` on **189**, against `bits` 2,823 / `onComplete` 105 / `itemChain` 27 / `targetMonsterKeys` 12 / `killGoals` 11 on the array side, plus 47 `battle:{…}` and one `finalBattle:{…}` on `NODE_MAP`. The path works — a bare `completion:{itemsAll:['…'],atNode:'LHR'}` and a four-level nest both serialize, patch and round-trip exactly when replayed by hand — so this is **coverage, not a red**; the point is that the one gate standing between §ARCH-01's entire quest shape and a silent write regression asserts the branch that has been *shrinking* since it was written. **Fix:** two checks in the existing file — patch a bare object field, reload, compare; patch a nested one, reload, compare — plus a line in the gate's header comment recording the §MATH-01 widening the way line 20 already records the §ARCH-01 `completeItems` repoint. **Related, not duplicate:** §DX-02h gave `WORLD_DB.monsters` its own writer precisely because the generic serializer is *wrong* there (it holds `P.<key>` code identifiers, not JSON) — that row is closed and its guard is separate. **Verify:** `npm run check:arraypatch` reports 15 checks and exits 0; both new checks fail against a stubbed `serializeJsLiteral` that drops object values (positive control).

### §DX-02ca — the MUD harness is red, `npm test:mud` exits 1, and the assertion that broke is the one that closed the §WALK series (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bm, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ca — a keep-alive that arrives late prunes the session it was sent to keep, and the harness gives it under 300 ms of margin.** 🟢 **one constant + three doc figures, ~20 minutes.** `npm run test:mud` exits **1** at HEAD — **267 passed / 2 failed**, deterministic across three consecutive runs — and the two failures are section **[D]**, the idle-TTL-prune property that §WALK-5 Inc 4 (`8a280af`) added to close the §WALK series: *"after idle TTL, exactly one session survives"* and *"Warm (kept active) survives; Ghost (idle) is pruned"*. **The prune is correct; the stopwatch is not.** Re-created in isolation the same scenario passes (one survivor, Warm); instrumented inside the full run, `/session/who` returns **count 0** — *both* sessions were reaped. `` `src/js/wbapi-server.js:function sessionPrune()@93` `` runs at the **top of every `/api/session/*` request**, before the `look` handler refreshes `lastSeen` — which is the behaviour you want, since a client silent for longer than the TTL *should* be pruned by its own late ping. But `src/tests/mud-harness.mjs` section [D] sets `TTL_MS = 700` with keep-alive `look`s on a 280 ms cadence, and by [D] the harness has five node servers live. **Measured round-trips in one instrumented run: 217 ms, 463 ms and 644 ms** — the second keep-alive was therefore issued **744 ms** after the first, past the TTL, so Warm's own keep-alive killed it. The margin has not changed since 2026-06-26; the harness has grown **11×** (24 → 269 assertions, 251 → 1,447 lines) around it. **Consequence beyond the red:** the `mud` job in `.github/workflows/walk-invariants.yml` is a *separate* CI job (`npm ci` + `test:mud`), so **CI fails on any push touching `src/js/mover.js`, `src/js/wbapi-server.js`, `play.html` or the harness itself** — and it is not in the recorded baseline-red set, so a future session will read it as their own regression. **Fix:** give [D] real headroom — raise its `TTL_MS` (3,000 ms costs ~2 s of wall clock) and/or touch Warm once immediately after `start` so the first interval is not the slow one; do **not** touch `sessionPrune`. **Also correct three live docs that record the harness as `269/269`** — `BACKLOG.md:147`, `potential.md:173`, `plan-archive.md` — all written before the red. `index.md:179`'s *"harness 24/24"* is historically correct for Inc 4 and stays. **Verify:** `npm run test:mud` exits 0 with 269/269, three consecutive runs; the [D] section still fails if `sessionPrune`'s selectivity is broken on purpose (positive control — pin the property, not the wall clock).

### §AUDIT-03be — six sites certify that Hunt Mode was removed, and Hunt Mode is live (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bl, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03be — §TIMELESS-01 deleted Hunt Mode on 2026-06-26; §KG-01 re-added it under the identical identifiers 12 days later, and nothing updated the paperwork of the first.** 🟢 **prose + one engine comment, ~15 minutes.** `7952752` (§TIMELESS-01 Inc B) removed the whole Hunt/Stalk subsystem and left ten labelled tombstone comments; `1d6263a` (Inc D) and `790d4f3` (the FU) swept the docs to match. Then `8168f0e` (§KG-01, 2026-07-08) shipped a **different** mechanic — `baseRate × 2` capped at 0.8 plus an 80/20 bias toward monsters at or below player level, no clock cost — reusing `huntMode`, `storyToggleHunt`, `_updateHuntBtn` and a 🎯 d-pad button verbatim. **The six false sites:** (1) the engine comment on line 38113, *"§TIMELESS-01: `_updateHuntBtn` / `storyToggleHunt` removed with the Hunt feature"*, which sits **39 and 28 lines below** `` `function _updateHuntBtn()@38270` `` and `` `function storyToggleHunt()@38281` `` — both live; (2) `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:690`, which lists `storyToggleHunt`, `_updateHuntBtn` and the `huntMode` state field under *"Removed code/state"*; (3) `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md:306`, *"no Hunt-Mode toggle — `S_story.huntMode` … removed in §TIMELESS-01"*; (4) `index.md:409`, the reverse-lookup row **Hunt Mode / stalk *(retired §TIMELESS-01)***; (5) `index.md:523`, *"Updated 2026-06-26 (§TIMELESS-01 removed `huntMode`)"*; and (6) **a separate defect in the same paragraph** — `mechanics-combat.md:688` states *"movement and battle no longer advance the clock"*, but **battle always did** by §TIMELESS-01's own D1 decision, and still does at `` `function _storyRollInit()@24689` `` (line 24650); the engine's own comment on line 28366 says only *movement* is timeless. **Only the Stalk half of the removal is real:** `storyQuestHunt`, `storyQuickWait`, `btn-hunt-toggle`, `btn-stalk-wait`, `btn-stalk-abandon`, `stalk-card` are all 0 occurrences, and `HUNTING_GROUNDS` / `_getQuestTargetKeys` / `_stalkedMonsterPick` survive only inside tombstones. **Fix:** rewrite tombstone 38113 to say the functions were removed by §TIMELESS-01 and reinstated by §KG-01 with different semantics (do not delete it — it is the only in-engine record of the first removal); split the four doc sites into a *retired (June)* / *live (July)* pair naming both mechanics; correct `:688` to *movement* only. **Related, not duplicate:** §AUDIT-03ba is stale prose beside correct code; this is prose that was correct when written and was falsified by a later commit reusing the vocabulary — the failure mode a two-way-sync rule cannot catch, because §KG-01 *did* document its own feature. **Verify:** no live doc or comment asserts `huntMode` is absent; a reader can tell the June mechanic from the July one. **⟶ EXTENDED 2026-08-14 (§DOC-02bm) — a SEVENTH site, already handled, and it is the most interesting of the seven.** `docs/lab-reports/lab-report-walk5-mud-harness.md` §8 said *"**No `huntMode`.** Anything in older notes implying a guaranteed-encounter mode is stale post-§TIMELESS-01"* — written **three hours** after §TIMELESS-01 shipped, by an author reading the file. It has been **annotated in place** by that report's §DOC-02bm rewrite, not added to this row's fix list. Two refinements it contributes: (a) the *server*-side half of the claim is still exactly true (`git log -S "s.huntMode" --all` returns **two commits, and both are lab reports** — the identifier was never in any engine), and (b) *"there is no guaranteed-encounter mode"* is **also still true**, because §KG-01's Hunt Mode is a doubling capped at 0.8, never a certainty. **Only the blanket sentence failed.** That is the shape to look for while sweeping the other six: the load-bearing error is usually a bare *"no X"* laid over a correct paragraph, not the paragraph.

### §AUDIT-03bf — `index.md` says it lists "All 193" state fields; the function has 493 and the table has 208 (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bl, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bf — the State Fields Quick Reference claims completeness it has never had, and the claim was wrong the day it was written.** 🟢 **one sentence.** `index.md:523` reads *"Moved from `index.md`. All 193 `S_story` fields from `_S_DEFAULTS()`. Updated 2026-06-26 (§TIMELESS-01 removed `huntMode`)."* By `git log -S`, that sentence entered the repo in **`1d6263a` — §TIMELESS-01 Inc D**, whose scope line included *"state-field count"*. **At that commit a brace-depth walk of `` `const _S_DEFAULTS = ()@23092` `` returns 486 top-level fields, and the doc's own table listed 205 `S_story.` rows.** 193 matched neither, so this is a **never-correct** figure rather than drift — the number appears to have been carried in rather than measured. At HEAD the walk returns **493** (375 of them corroborated by an `S_story.<key>` read elsewhere in the file; the remainder are flag fields reached through the gate compiler or dynamic indexing) and the table lists **208** rows. **The load-bearing error is the word *"All"***: the reference has never covered more than ~42 % of `_S_DEFAULTS()`, and a reader who trusts it will conclude a field is undeclared when it simply is not tabulated. **Fix:** replace with a measured, self-dating sentence — *"the N most commonly referenced of the M fields in `_S_DEFAULTS()`; not exhaustive"* — and, since both numbers rot, prefer pointing at `npm run stats` or adding a field count to it over hardcoding M. **Note the second half of the same line is also now false** — *"§TIMELESS-01 removed `huntMode`"* — and is covered by §AUDIT-03be; fix both in one edit. **Instrument 66 in its home habitat:** the one figure an author copies out of a *document* instead of the *file* is the one that is wrong, and a count beside the words "All" and a function name reads as measured whether or not it was. **Verify:** the sentence states a measured count or defers to a generator, and does not claim exhaustiveness.

### §AUDIT-03bd — CI gate #1 counts 110 of the world's 111 terrains, because its key pattern cannot see a quoted key (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bk, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03bd — `check:invariants` is one authored node away from a false red on the gate that proves the world is walkable.** 🟢 **one regex, ~5 minutes.** `src/scripts/check-invariants.js:127` collects `WORLD_DB`'s top-level terrain keys with `` /^ {2}([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*:/ `` — bare identifiers only. `WORLD_DB` holds **111** entries by a brace-depth walk and by `npm run stats`; the gate reports **110**. The missing one is the single quoted key, `'bar (Visby)':` — the Broken Tooth Tavern terrain. **Nothing is red today** because no node currently carries `name:'bar (Visby)'`, so I1's *"node references a terrain absent from `WORLD_DB`"* check finds nothing to complain about. Author one node onto that terrain and gate #1 fails, blaming the node. **The tell that this is a known class and was guarded on the other side only:** the very next scan in the same file — the NODE_MAP terrain pass at `src/scripts/check-invariants.js:142` — carries an explicit staleness guard (*"only parsed N node terrains for M coord'd nodes — regex likely stale"*). The WORLD_DB side has no such guard, so its blindness is silent by construction. **Fix:** widen the pattern to `` /^ {2}('[^']+'|[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*:/ `` and strip the quotes, then add the same count-sanity guard the NODE_MAP side already has (fail if the parsed key count diverges from a brace-depth count). **Instrument 51 in a live CI gate:** a census is only as good as the pattern that took it, and the entry written in the *older* style — the one quoted key, dating to the era when terrain keys carried spaces — is the one the newer pattern cannot see.

### §AUDIT-03aw — two whole shipped arcs, 15 quests and the game's only companion have no row in any maintained home doc (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02as, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§AUDIT-03aw — the two-way-sync rule failed silently for §WISDOM-01 and §ALCHEMY-01, and the index still files one of them as future work.** prompt.md §2 step 6 and CONTRIBUTING's two-way-sync rule are hard: *"every changed structure updates its home doc in the same increment."* Measured this pass: **`quest.md` contains 0 of `quest_wis_00`–`_07` and 0 of `quest_alch_01`–`_07`** — 15 shipped UQF quests with no register row — and **`quest.md`, `story.md`, `world.md` and `mechanics.md` return 0 hits each for `Roen`, `Ardley` and `ALCHEMY-01`.** This is not a general gap in `quest.md` (934 lines): its siblings from the same fortnight are all there — `quest_vs_01` ×1, `quest_wm_01` ×1, `quest_spark_05` ×3, `quest_courier_release` ×3. **`index.md:175` is the sharpest single line:** `| **§WISDOM-01** | — | Keel thread close: Baltic survey data arc at eastern Baltic node; "after witnessing" arc completion |` — a *planned-work* row, in the present tense, for an arc that shipped **2026-05-28** at `e339aeb` and whose Keel thread (`quest_wis_03`) has been live for 77 days. `index.md:399` then points the reader at `plan-archive.md §WISDOM-01` — **the arc's only record is in the HISTORY file**, so the shipping event and the archival happened without the register in between.
> **Why it matters more than a missing row:** Roen is the game's **only companion character** and these two arcs are his whole through-line (self-knowledge → reading other people). A cold agent greping `world.md` for the companion system finds nothing, and `quest.md` — the location-organized register prompt.md names as the home doc for quests — reports that Tilbury, Saltwick and the Intercept have no wisdom content on them. That is the §AUDIT-03z thesis from the other side: *a bijection guarantees a link, not a truth* — here **there is no link at all**, which is the one shape a doc-anchor gate cannot fail on, because a missing row has no anchor to resolve.
> **Fix (🟢, no design call):** add the 15 rows to `quest.md` under their live nodes (`LCY`/`MME`/`GCI`/`BK`→`VBY`/`ATH`/`VS`/`KIR`), a §Roen section to `world.md`, and correct `index.md:175` from a planned row to a shipped one. **Mark the reachability honestly while you do it** — §DOC-02u's §SIREN-01 lesson is that `quest.md` saying *"✅ LIVE"* about a quest no player can reach is worse than silence, and all 8 §WISDOM-01 quests plus 5 of 7 §ALCHEMY-01 quests are currently unreachable (§AUDIT-03x). **Measure the corpus before closing:** two arcs found while verifying one report is not a search — census every `QUEST_DB` id prefix against `quest.md` and report the full set of unregistered arcs, which is a two-line script and the natural seed for a `check:questdoc` phase of §DX-02s's proposed `check:docindex`.

### §DX-02w — no gate can see an `activateNode` that resolves but can never be stood on (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02r)

- [ ] **§DX-02w — `check:cellprimacy`, the gate phase §AUDIT-03x has been asking for since 2026-08-11.** Every existing gate passes on a co-located node: `check:noderegs` proves the code *resolves* in `NODE_MAP`, and it does; `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself; `check:legacycodes` scans `.md` only. Nothing tests the property that actually matters — whether `S_story.currentCode` can ever hold that code. `const CELL_GRID = (() => {@9865` builds each cell in `NODE_MAP` declaration order and only `list[0]` is reachable, because `currentCode` is assigned at exactly two sites (`S_story.currentCode = destCode;@28525`, `S_story.checkpointNode || 'LHR'@26142`).
> **Wanted:** a phase that fails on any `activateNode` / `waypointNode` / `node.code === '…'` guard naming a code that is not `list[0]` in its cell, with **explicit classification** (a `CO_LOCATED_BY_DESIGN` list with a reason per entry) in the #13/#14/#16 house style — a percentage heuristic is blind to a cell that is *entirely* non-primary, and cell `32,203` holds **17 nodes**.
> **Second phase worth the same pass — the transitive one, and §DOC-02r's 19th instrument:** a quest on a *live* node can still be unreachable when its gate flag has exactly one writer and that writer sits inside a blocked node's `storyRender` block. `quest_wis_01`/`_02`/`_03` sit on live `LCY`/`MME`/`GCI` and are dead behind `S_story.wisHookReceived = true;@33729`. **A single-writer census plus the primacy map finds these; nothing else does.**
> **Baseline for the gate when it lands:** 416 nodes / 244 cells / 172 non-primary (§AUDIT-03x, re-confirmed — and re-confirmed a **third** time by §DOC-02u's independent closure, which reproduced all three figures exactly).
> **§AUDIT-03x extended by §DOC-02z — cell `10,191` is the second cell that must be scored BY CELL, and the layer's hub is a rest node nobody can stand on.** The naval layer's four blocked hosts are `SEN`←`LCY` (cell 18,180), `HFT` and `VAW`←`ALF` (cell 10,191), `ATH`←`SEA` (32,203). **Cell `10,191` holds 6 nodes from 2 independent tracks and strands 5**: the whole lake sub-map — `ALF` *North Shore Path* (primary), `HVG` *East Coast — Upper Shore*, `HFT` *South Shore — The Fishermen's Village*, `VAW` *North Shore Den* — **plus `ODD` *Oddrun's Estate* and `SIG` *Siggeir's Hall*, two Völsunga-saga nodes with no relation to the lake.** That is §DOC-02x's *"change the unit — score by cell, not by arc"* rule reproduced in a second cell. **`SEN` *Aboard the Tilbury Star* is the sharpest single node measured so far**: `SEN:{ num:10, code:'SEN'@8651` carries **8 `activateNode` quests**, `battle:{label:'Pirate ×3 + Ghost'`, `loot:'Cargo Manifest'`, `npc:'Ship Captain'` **and `sleep:true, sleepCost:3` — so it is a Warrant's Board host** (`function _boardHost(node)@37241` keys on `node.sleep`) **that no player can ever reach.** Its 8 quests are §WHODUNIT-01's four, §SPARK-01 SEA's head, §ALCHEMY-01's ship beat, and **`quest_spark_03` + `quest_spark_04` — two beats of §SPARK-01 itself**, so the parent arc is a casualty too. Third occupant of 18,180 is `STN`, the Map Shop that carries §AUDIT-03u's *"forty-two nodes"* string. **New this pass: §ALCHEMY-01 loses 5 of 7** (`roenAtSea`'s sole writer is `if (node.code === 'SEN' && S_story.roenMidlandsWisdom && !S_story.roenAtSea) {@33595`), and **the §BOARD-01-FU6 referral graph loses its one geo-spanning DIAMOND APEX** — `quest_hunt_01` can activate and can never complete, so `{ kind:'unlock', quests:['sq_2','quest_hunt2_01'] }` in `quest_hunt_01@12903` never fires (both arms survive independently; what is lost is the sentence that makes them one pattern).

> **§AUDIT-03x — CHEAPEST INSTANCE IDENTIFIED 2026-08-12 (§DOC-02ai), and it needs no design call.** `ATH`←`SEA` (cell **`32,203`**, the 17-node cell) has been on this row since §DOC-02r/§DOC-02z as one line in a casualty list; §DOC-02ai measured what it costs and found the fix is **two lines, not a locale decision**. Both nodes belong to the SAME authored block, **ten lines apart** — `` `SEA:{ num:97, code:'SEA'@8490` `` is declared before `` `ATH:{ num:92, code:'ATH'@8500` ``, and `CELL_GRID` builds each cell in `NODE_MAP` declaration order, so the arc's own **sea** node is the primary of Athens's cell. The bill: `` `quest_areopagus: { id:'quest_areopagus'@11476` `` carries `activateNode:'ATH'` and **cannot activate**, so the Areopagus speech — the §FUTURE-01 arc's intellectual climax and the only scene in it calibrated for a non-Jewish audience — never fires; `ATH` is `sleep:true, sleepCost:5`, a **checkpoint that can never be set**; and a player who walks to Athens arrives at *"The Inner Sea"*, a shipboard scene, on dry land in Achaia. **Unlike every other member of this row, repointing is not the question and co-location is not load-bearing** — nothing wants a sea node and a city on one cell; swapping the two declarations (or giving `SEA` its own water-adjacent cell) restores Athens without touching the locale design. **Do this one ahead of the gate.**
> **And the closure runs in the POSITIVE direction too — §DOC-02z's method note.** Before writing a quest off, check `unlock` edges *and* the Warrant's Board: `_bountyPostable` never tests primacy, so a board-postable quest whose completion lands on a **primary** node is fully playable. That is what keeps §SPARK-01 SEA alive (`quest_sea_01` gate `{}`, `xp:100` under the Unknown tier's 250 cap, `completion:{ atNode:'NWI' }`). **The gate must therefore report a stranded node's quests as *board-only*, not as dead** — and it depends on §DX-02ah, which is the surface that makes the board route work at all.
> **§AUDIT-03x extended by §DOC-02u — the FIRST 100 % CASUALTY, and the case that most argues for this gate.** §SIREN-01 (the Littoral Courts) loses **6 of 6 surviving nodes and 5 of 5 quests**: `LC1`/`LC2`/`LC3`/`LC4`/`LSO` are the 3rd–7th occupants of cell `32,203` (17 nodes, `list[0]` = `SEA` — the same cell §DOC-02r found stranding `ATH`), and `LCA:{r:35,c:213},`@9825 is 2nd of 2 behind `CI2`. Previous casualties left *something* standing — §CROWN-01 kept WG0 and INN reachable out of 9 nodes, §DOC-02r's prosocial family kept 23 of 51 quests. **Here there is no entry point, no partial experience and no arc-close: `{ id:'story-lca-close', nodes:['LCA'],@31585` cannot render either, so the arc cannot even end.** ***And nothing in the repo says so*** — `quest.md` lists all five as **"✅ LIVE §SIREN-01"**, which is true of `QUEST_DB` and false of the game. That is precisely the true/false split this gate exists to make legible, and it is the strongest single argument for building it: the arc is 100 % dead, every gate is green, and the docs say it works.

### §DX-02ad — `quest.md` calls itself the register of *all* quests and names 148 of 2,853 (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02x, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02ad — a bijection that shipped, was declared canonical, and quietly stopped being true; §DOC-02i's FC01 class in a second subject.** `quest.md`'s own Purpose line reads *"Location-organized register of **all quests** — implemented, planned, and specced,"* and `lab-report-meta-process-loop-expansion.md` recommendation 8 is where the claim was written down (*"The register is the canonical source of truth for quest IDs, locations, and act structure"*). Measured through the canonical `wbapi-core` parse — the same extractor `:1367` and every `src/scripts/check-*.js` use: **`QUEST_DB` holds 2,853 entries across 57 id prefixes; `quest.md` names 148 of them (5.2 %).** Broken down: the hand-authored `quest_*` family is **145 of 298 (48.7 %)**, and the other **56 families hold 2,555 quests of which 3 are named** (`ada` 235 · `ath` 120 · `lis` 95 · `zth` 80 · `flr` 75 · `blq` 60 · `kya` 60 · `hft`/`rkv`/`ost`/`rix`/`jrs` 55 each · …). **⟶ SECOND SUBJECT, SAME SHAPE, 2026-08-12 (§DOC-02ab).** `docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md` headed its transcript *"Source: `NPC_DIALOGUES` const … 6 NPCs × 4 states × 5 quotes = 120 lines"* while `const NPC_DIALOGUES = {@10410` holds **213 entries and 1,614 quotes** — a complete and accurate transcript of **2.8 %** of the table, wrong only by omission, so every spot-check passes. Its per-NPC arithmetic was off too: all six Birka pools are 5/5/5/**4**, so the true figure is **114**, not 120. Both corrected in place. **So the gate this row wants should be scoped to *any doc that declares itself the register of a named data section*, not to `quest.md` alone.** Cheap general form: if a doc's own prose names a `const` **and** a count, re-derive the count from that `const` through `wbapi-core` and fail on a mismatch.
> **The reverse direction is SPOTLESS, and that is why nobody has caught it.** Thirteen quest-shaped tokens in `quest.md` fail to resolve in `QUEST_DB` and **not one is a dead row**: ten are deliberate annotations of the form `` `quest_d0207_a1–a5` *(design: quest_cy_madness_gate)* `` — live id first, design-era name preserved beside it, which is the same discipline that let §DOC-02k score §DUNGEON-01 rather than guess at it — and the other three are prose fragments. ***So the register is wrong only by OMISSION and never by ASSERTION: everything it says is true, and it says it about one quest in nineteen.*** That is the hardest staleness to notice, because every spot-check passes — the §DOC-02i mechanism exactly (*a check that depends on a human running it reports green when nobody ran it*).
> **Fix — the cheapest gate in the family, a two-way `comm` over the parser's own key list.** `check:questindex`: `WBAPI.load()` → `Object.keys(questDb)` vs the ids `quest.md` cites. Two explicit classification lists, both in the §AUDIT-03j house style rather than a heuristic: **`NOT_A_QUEST_ID`** for the `*(design: …)*` aliases and prose fragments, and a **generated-family exemption list** naming each bulk prefix with a reason, so the register is scored against the population it is actually meant to cover. **The exemption must be written down, not inferred** — a percentage heuristic is blind to a family that is *entirely* generated, which is the same argument gates #13/#14/#16 each had to make.
> **Then decide the register's scope in one sentence and put it in `quest.md`'s Purpose line**, because "all quests" has not been true since the bulk families landed and the doc has never said so.

### §DX-02br — `navigation.test.js` suppresses encounters by stubbing the RNG the code stopped drawing (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02az, 🟢 no design call)

- [ ] **§DX-02br — the third recurrence of the same lesson, in the file that was rebuilt to fix the second.** `src/tests/integration/navigation.test.js`'s `moveNoEncounter(page, dir)` helper wraps `cellMove` in `Math.random = () => 1` with the comment *"Suppress the per-step terrain encounter roll (Math.random >= rate → no battle)"*, and the auto-travel block pins `Math.random = () => 0.999999` for the same purpose. **The roll is `if (_seededNext() < baseRate)` inside `function _enterEmptyCell(r, c)@28575`** — §VM-01-B moved it onto the seeded stream, and `function _seededNext()@6436` draws `S_story.rngState` through mulberry32 and **never touches `Math.random`**. The stub controls nothing. It is also restored synchronously after `cellMove`, while the battle fires from a `setTimeout(…, 300)`, so it would be ineffective even if it were the right stream. The affected tests pass today only because the terrain rates involved are 0.05–0.25 and the assertions land before the timer.
> **Blast radius:** every `moveNoEncounter` call site (empty-cell shell, room layer, D-pad, sea-lane walk) plus *"WP click travels NUE → MGR (3 cells)"* and *"Shift+WP takes a single step"*. The one test that genuinely does suppress is *"a wilderness encounter halts auto-travel"* — it forces `TERRAIN_ENCOUNTER_RATE[k] = 1`, which works regardless of stream. **Fix:** suppress through the engine's own dial the way §DX-02f did — set the rates to 0 (or seed `S_story.rngState` to a value whose next draw is known) — never by stubbing a global the code no longer reads. ***A test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws*** (§DX-02e pinned generated coordinates, §DX-02f stubbed the wrong stream, this is the third). Also in the same file: the comment *"The invariant suite proves full reachability offline (409/409)"* is stale — the figure is **416/416**.

### §DX-02bu — the §DATA-01-REVERTED row's "zero functions" claim is false (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02ba, 🟢 doc correction)

- [ ] **§DX-02bu — a closure row that closed itself with a number nobody re-measured.** §DATA-01-REVERTED (2026-07-06) closed the row as superseded on the evidence that UQF quests are *"declarative UQF-1.0 — `onPass`/`onFail` grep to 2,650+ occurrences but **zero functions** (all `[{kind:…}]` descriptor arrays)."* Measured at HEAD: there are **124 `{kind:'_legacy_fn', fn:…}` descriptors, every one carrying an inline anonymous arrow function**, all of them inside `const QUEST_DB = {@10630`. **118 take no argument and 41 of those name `S_story` directly** — closures over live game state, the exact construct §DATA-01 was opened to remove. The claim was false when written.
> **The decision it supported still stands** — 124 of **5,638** descriptors is **2.2 %**, against §ARCH-02's `{e:'hook'}` share of **107 of 122 = 87.7 %**, so UQF really did reach the goal and rebuilding `QUEST_EFFECTS` really would have been a second competing system. Only the *evidence sentence* is wrong, and it is wrong in the direction that makes the system look finished. **Fix:** amend the row to *"124 `_legacy_fn` closures remain (2.2 %), each tagged and probed by `check:questgraph`"*. **Not in conflict with §DOC-02b's *"16 `_legacyFn` left"*** — that is the camelCase kernel identifier (17 occurrences), a different symbol from the snake_case descriptor kind. ***Two spellings one underscore apart, counted separately in two rows, is how a corpus disagrees with itself without either author being careless.***

### §DX-02bv — 175 line-numbered doc rows that `check:anchors` is structurally unable to see (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02bb, 🟡)

- [ ] **§DX-02bv — zero dead anchors and 167 stale line references, in the same files, on the same run.** Six maintained home docs carry function-reference tables in `| Function | Line |` two-column form: `docs/spec/combat.md` (55 rows) · `world.md` (29) · `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md` (29) · `story.md` (27) · `mechanics.md` (26) · `monsters.md` (9) — **175 rows total, 0 exact, 167 stale, 8 unresolvable.** A 0 % hit rate. Sampled drift on `docs/spec/combat.md` runs **+934 to +1,284 lines**; every claimed line now lands inside `MONSTER_POOL` data rather than the function it names (`roll(sides)` claimed 5483, actual 6417; `syncCharFromUI()` claimed 6743, actual 8027).
> **The gate built for exactly this cannot see any of it.** `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js:const DOC_ROOTS@43` already walks `.`, `docs` and `lab-reports`, so all six files are in scope, and `check:anchors` passes clean at **2,730 anchors across 60 docs, 0 dead** — because `src/scripts/resolve-anchors.js:const ANCHOR_RE@48` matches only `` `symbol@line` `` **inside backticks**. The two-column table form is not *stale* to the gate; it is **invisible**. This is the §DX-02n family's shape from a third side: not a census with zero readers, but a **doc-drift class outside the scan universe** — same lesson as §BOARD-01's flag-browser prior art, *widen the scan universe rather than start over*.
> **Fix (mechanical, no design call):** convert each `| Line |` column to the anchor form (`` `function roll(sides)@6418` ``) so the gate owns them, then `npm run anchors:fix` to refresh. Alternative if the column must stay: teach `ANCHOR_RE` a second pattern for `| `sym` | NNNN |` rows. **Sub-item (🟢):** `index.md`'s footer hardcodes four live totals and two have already drifted — **37,950 vs 38,712 lines** and **~2,848 vs 2,853 quests** (416 nodes and 398 monsters still exact). `npm run stats` exists precisely so these are not hardcoded, and prompt.md §0 already says so.
> **Provenance:** this is the `lab-report-synthesis-part1-architecture.md` §API-03 / SP5 prediction of **2026-06-16**, confirmed 58 days later at **175× the scope it named** (it named one table in one document). Zero commits in the repo mention SP5 or §API-03. The report was right that the numbers would rot; nobody was wrong about the fix, because nobody looked.

### §DX-02bx — Yael's Named Report scene: the code says Act VI, its own comment and `world.md` say Act IV (NEW 2026-08-14 during §DOC-02bf, 🟢 doc correction)

- [ ] **§DX-02bx — the engine comment and the maintained home doc are both wrong about the same gate, and a lab report is the only source that kept it.** The live trigger is `yaelNamedReportDelivered) {@32706`, guarded by `_npcFavor('yael') >= 2 && (S_story.actNumber || 1) >= 6 && S_story.yaelEscortUsed` at node `LHR`. Two sources disagree with it: (1) `const YAEL_NAMED_REPORT_SCENE = {@28189` — the const's **own header comment** — reads *"Layer 74; fav >= 2 Act IV+; yaelNamedReportDelivered"*, understating the act gate by two; (2) `world.md:617` says the scene *"fires at a `LLA` or `HKG` visit (fav_yael ≥ 2, Act IV+, `yaelNamedReportFired` not set)"* — **wrong node** (live gate is `LHR`), **wrong act**, and **`yaelNamedReportFired` is a phantom: 1 occurrence in `world.md`, 0 in `play.html`.**
> **Fix (mechanical, no design call):** correct the header comment to Act VI+, and correct `world.md:617` to `LHR` / Act VI+ / `yaelNamedReportDelivered`. Delete the phantom `yaelNamedReportFired` — it has never existed in the engine, so nothing can be reading it.
> **Why it matters beyond the numbers:** an author trusting either source would test the scene in Act IV, see nothing fire, and conclude the arc is broken. The scene is fine; the map to it is not. Act VI is also the correct number *narratively* — Yael files her second report late, after the player has seen the archive suppression, and moving the gate to IV would land the beat before its setup.
> **Provenance:** §DOC-02bf. Distinct from §DOC-02y's §AUDIT-03x finding on the same scene, which covered the **patrol addendum's** node (`nodeSlug:'MSY'@27870`) and cell primacy, not the act gate. ***A lab report outscoring both the engine comment and the maintained home doc inverts the program's usual direction — instrument 6 both ways: the archive-facing document is not always the stale one.***

### §DX-02bw — §DX-02n's `q.xpAward` sub-item says "0 readers"; there are two (NEW 2026-08-13 during §DOC-02bc, 🟢 evidence correction)

- [ ] **§DX-02bw — a dead-field finding filed against a live field, and the engine's own comment names the other one.** §DX-02n (extended by §DOC-02e) records `q.xpAward` as *"53 occurrences, 33 on UQF quests, **0 readers** — the undocumented twin of `q.reward`."* Measured at HEAD: **two readers.** (1) The quest-completion loop — `// §D02 xpAward on side-type quest completion@30358`, `if (q.type === 'side' && q.xpAward) { S_story.xp += q.xpAward; _checkLevelUp(); }` — shipped **2026-05-26 in `8abc606`, a commit whose entire subject is *"ui stuff"***. (2) The Warrant's Board honest-reward preview, whose own comment calls it *"the live payout consumed at storyCheckQuests"* and states that **`q.reward` is intentionally NOT read — it is a dead/display-only field**. So the pair is real and the polarity in the row is reversed.
> **Scope measured:** **51 `QUEST_DB` entries carry `xpAward`, and 46 of them are `type:'side'`** — the exact shape reader (1) requires; the other 5 are `skill_check` quests whose XP comes from a `reward` bit, so for those the field genuinely is inert. **Fix:** correct the §DX-02n sub-item's evidence sentence (the field stays), and if `check:deadconsts` is ever built, this is the case that proves the census must be **per-consumer-shape**, not per-symbol: a field read behind `q.type === 'side'` is live for 46 entries and dead for 5, in the same table, under the same name.
> **Not in conflict with the rest of §DX-02n** — `LOOT_TABLE` (0 readers) and `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted` (write-only) were re-confirmed dead in this same pass. ***A "0 readers" claim is a search result, and a search that misses one line inverts the verdict for the whole field.***

- [ ] **§DX-02j — 53 more `MONSTER_POOL` entries are authored content nothing can reach (measured 2026-08-03 during §DX-02h).** Scanning all 398 pool keys for a single reference outside `MONSTER_POOL`/`MONSTER_DROPS`, **55 have none** — no `WORLD_DB` roster, no `node.battle`, no quest, no `EPIC_BOSS_POOL`. §DX-02h placed 2 (`void_rat_swarm`, `void_wolf`); **53 remain**, each with a stat block and a trophy drop that no player can ever earn. They are not a deliberate reserve: ~50 sit in the **Dark Fantasy Bestiary** block (`play.html` `// ── Dark Fantasy Bestiary`), which is *partially* rostered — `fleder`, `cave_bear`, `koshchey`, `shaelmaar`, `ysbaddaden`, `garkain`, `slyzard` are orphaned while their immediate neighbours `protofleder`, `warg`, `alp`, `basilisk` are live — so this is an import that was wired up halfway, not a plan. The rest: `lemure`, `unicorn`, `black_bear`, `void_warlord`, `slyzard_matriarch`, `dragon_of_fyresdal`, `bruxa_corvo_bianco`. **Decide per group, not per monster** (rostering 53 one at a time is 53 design calls): the deadly named four read as boss/set-piece material wanting a `node.battle` or a quest, while the medium/hard bestiary bulk wants one or two terrain rosters. **The durable half is a gate**, and it needs the corpus clean first: a `check:monsterreach` in the §AUDIT-03j/n mould — **explicit** classification, an unlisted orphan FAILS — so a monster authored tomorrow cannot go unreachable silently. Note the trap that hid this class: `world.md` described a runtime injection (`_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`) that **does not exist**, so the doc made two orphans look rostered; re-measure, never read placement off a doc (§AUDIT-03l). **Risk:** low per roster write (`./api.sh put terrain … monsters=` now round-trips, §DX-02h), but the encounter-weight balance of a terrain changes with every addition — add a few, eyeball §7½, do not bulk-dump 50 into one roster.

- [ ] **§DX-02n — two engine structures with ZERO readers, one of them carrying a doc comment that names a reader it does not have (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02b).** The §DX-02j class in a second dimension: not unreachable *content*, but unread *code and state*. **Two measured members.** (a) **`LOOT_TABLE` is dead data that documents itself as live.** `` `const LOOT_TABLE` *(deleted 2026-08-26, §DROP-01-FU)* `` declares a full d100 loot table with the trailing note *"→ doc: docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md §D100 Loot Table (d100 result → item; used by `_rollD100Loot()`)"* — and the identifier `LOOT_TABLE` occurs **exactly once in the entire 38,707-line file**: on that declaration line. `_rollD100Loot()` reads `_D100_TABLE` (`` `const _D100_TABLE@24576` ``) instead. So the comment asserts a caller that does not exist, which is strictly worse than no comment, because the note is what a reader greps for and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md` may be documenting the wrong
  table on its strength. **First job is to decide which table is canonical** — compare `LOOT_TABLE`'s rows against `_D100_TABLE`'s before deleting either; if they disagree, the *shipped* loot curve is `_D100_TABLE`'s and the doc needs re-anchoring, not the code. (b) **`S_story.ebReturnsCompleted` is a write-only shadow of `ebReturnDone`.** Both are initialised in `_S_DEFAULTS()` (`` `ebReturnsCompleted: {}` *(deleted, §DX-02eo)* ``, `` `ebReturnDone: {}@23116` ``) and both are written by the same function on adjacent lines (`` `S_story.ebReturnDone[ebCode] = true@30521` `` and `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted[ebCode] = true` at 30360). `ebReturnDone` has **12 read sites** — `_curseScore()`, `_missionComplete()`, the victory screen, the node tint, and the 20 `quest_e*_return` completions via `flagsPath:['ebReturnDone.XXX']`. `ebReturnsCompleted` has **zero**, and persists into every save file meaning nothing. **This is the quietest form of Hazard #2** — *a write into a real-but-wrong object
  never throws* — and here it is a write into a real-but-**unread** object, so even the round-trip acceptance test hazard #5 prescribes would pass it green: the field saves, reloads, and is still never consulted. **The durable half is a gate**, the §AUDIT-03j/n house style: a `check:deadconsts` phase that flags a top-level `const`, or a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field, whose identifier occurs only at its own declaration/write sites, with **explicit** classification (a deliberate reserve must be listed with a reason) so an unlisted one FAILS. Measure the corpus first — the two found here were found by hand while verifying an unrelated report, which is not a search. **Needs no design call for (b)** (delete the field + its two writes; it is unread by construction). **(a) needs a one-line call** once the two tables are diffed: delete `LOOT_TABLE`, or repoint `_rollD100Loot()` at it. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** for (b), grep proves 0 readers before and after, and a save round-trip is unchanged; for
  (a), a diff of the two tables recorded in the row, then `npm test`'s loot pins. **⟶ TWO MORE MEMBERS, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02h), both §CROWN-01 Amendment A.** (c) **`waneStoneDCReduced`** — one writer (`` `set:['waneStoneDCReduced']@12009` ``, `quest_wane_spawn.onComplete`), one declaration (`` `waneStoneDCReduced: false@23234` ``), **no third site in 38,707 lines**. Its lab report specifies it as *"future attempts on quest_wane_02 use DC:10"* — and `quest_wane_02` ships `retryable:false`, so there is no retry to discount even if a reader existed. A field written to record a rule the engine has no way to apply. (d) **`Marsh Seaweed`** — a *drop item*, not a field: it occurs **exactly once in the file**, at its own push (`` `name:'Marsh Seaweed'@7073` ``, from 5 monster keys), carrying `desc:'Mire seaweed. Burns to trace iodine salt at the inn hearth.'` **Nothing consumes it** — `quest_iodine_02`/`_03` require `Swamp Kelp` only. So it is vendor trash whose own description names a
  craft that does not exist (it is therefore also an §AUDIT-03v-cluster instance). **This widens the proposed `check:deadconsts` scope a THIRD time** — after `q.xpAward` (quest-entry fields) and `_setActivePath`'s dead `toCode` (parameters), now **item objects pushed to `S_story.inventory` whose `name` is never read back**. Neither (c) nor (d) needs a design call for deletion; (d) has the alternative of *giving* it a consumer, which is the more likely intent. **⟶ A FIFTH MEMBER, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02i), and it widens the scope a FOURTH time — to a dead alternative inside a fallback chain.** (e) **`visitedNodes`** — the activation-gate reader is `` `st.visited || st.visitedNodes@22078` ``, and `visitedNodes` occurs **exactly once in 38,712 lines**, in that expression. It has **one commit in the file's entire history**, and it is not a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field at HEAD *or* in the 2026-05-24 archive build. The live field is `visited`. So the right-hand branch is unreachable and always was:
  defensive code written for a field that was never declared. **A top-level scan cannot see this and neither can a field scan** — the identifier appears only as an operand, so `check:deadconsts` needs a phase for *alternatives in a `||` chain whose identifier has no declaration*. Deleting the branch is a no-op by construction; **no design call.** **⟶ (a) IS NOW MEASURED AND ITS DESIGN CALL IS ANSWERED, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02j) — the two tables were diffed and `_D100_TABLE` is unambiguously canonical.** `LOOT_TABLE` is a **d20** table (20 entries: 8 Minor · 2 Spell Scroll · 5 Healing · 3 Greater · 2 Superior — its own doc comment calling it a *d100* table is a third error in that one line); `_D100_TABLE` is 7 weight-100 rows including flashbang and gold, which `LOOT_TABLE` has no concept of. **The shipped loot curve is `_D100_TABLE`'s, so the fix is delete `LOOT_TABLE`, not repoint anything.** Expected potion healing differs materially — **27.75 HP/kill** under the dead table vs
  **21.0 HP/kill** live — so anything balanced against the doc was balanced against the wrong curve. **Two doc-side defects fall out, and they are the reason this is worth more than a deletion.** (i) **Both home docs already claim the array is gone.** `docs/mechanics/mechanics-economy.md:253` says *"`LOOT_TABLE` | (removed) | Old d20 drop table — replaced by `_D100_TABLE` (§DROP-01 2026-06-05); definition now a comment stub"* and `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md:193` says *"`LOOT_TABLE` (old d20 array, Layer 25) removed; replaced by comment stub."* **There is no comment stub — HEAD still declares all 20 entries.** So two docs record a cleanup that never happened, which is the inverse of the usual rot and strictly more dangerous: a reader who greps the doc concludes the code is already clean. (ii) The archive kills the doc's own date — `LOOT_TABLE` had **exactly one occurrence at `32c10c5` (2026-05-24) too**, so it was already unread **twelve days before** the §DROP-01 commit
  the doc credits with replacing it. *(§DOC-02g's rule again: a migration note is a claim about the past, written from memory rather than from the diff.)* **Still no design call** — delete the array, delete the false pointer comment, correct both doc rows to *"removed 2026-08-xx"*. **⟶ SIX MORE MEMBERS FROM ONE FEATURE, 2026-08-11 (§DOC-02k, §DUNGEON-01) — and one of them argues the gate must run the census in BOTH directions.** All six are `_S_DEFAULTS()` fields with writers and **zero readers**: (f) `voidFluxImmunityChoice` — one writer, always the literal `'fire'`, and it is the **entire reward** of `quest_d0209_a2`, a DC 12 Arcana check whose passText promises *"One spell effect will behave normally in this chamber"*; (g) `` `S_story.spiritDefeated = false;@21822` `` — **a `_legacy_fn` that sets a defeat flag to `false` at the moment of victory**, which is the sharpest member in the whole row because it is inert either way, so **no test and no gate can distinguish the bug
  from the intent**; (h) `mazeSolvedChecks` — three writers stepping 1 → 2 → 3, no reader, and the third writer is unreachable anyway (§AUDIT-03y); (i) `voidMazeEntered` — one `flag_write`, no reader, likewise unreachable; (j) `tribbleGladesFed` — one writer, no reader; (k) `cyMadnessTable` — one writer, no reader, and it directly contradicts its own lab report's §4, which states that `cyMadnessRoll` is *"the only persistent effect"* of failing the save. **The direction-widening point belongs to the sibling row §DX-02u:** `voidFluxCleared` is the **mirror image** of every member above — three readers and **no writer** — so it is not dead weight but a **broken dependency**, and a `check:deadconsts` scoped to *unread* identifiers walks straight past it. Whatever this gate ends up being, it must census **readers and writers separately and fail on either being zero**, with the gate-leaf flag names in `QUEST_DB` as the cheapest corpus to run it over (every one is a string literal).

  was **specified**: `lab-report-npc-dialogue-system.md` §IV lists `S_story.ebReturnsCompleted >= 20` as a mission bit, and the engine duly writes it beside `S_story.ebReturnDone[ebCode] = true;@30521` — while `allEbReturns: Object.keys(NPC_DIALOGUES).length > 0@23688` reads the **sibling**. ***So a dead field usually has a document behind it, and the document usually reads as a requirement — which is why deleting one should be preceded by `grep`ping the lab reports for its name.*** Two more, both in `_missionComplete`: (p) that same `allEbReturns` line opens with a conjunct over an **immutable non-empty literal**, so it is always true and guards nothing — a *dead guard* rather than a dead field, invisible to a reader/writer census because both operands are live; and (q) `journalHalf: (S_story.journalEntriesRead || []).length >= 9,@23689` is named *half* while asking for 9 of `FROBERGER_JOURNAL`'s 41 entries — **22 %** — a bit whose NAME is the only documentation of its threshold

Either give it a reader (an NPC row, a favour tier, or a `flags:['silarJoined']` gate on the second-journey
  quests, which is what the arc's own structure implies) or delete it — but **decide, because a companion flag with no consumer is indistinguishable from one whose consumer was lost.** **+1 (2026-08-12, §DOC-02ak): `baitFishingActive` — one occurrence in 38,712 lines, its own declaration at `tackleboxZoneUnlocks: {shore:true@23140`.** Never written, never read; the *sibling* field declared on the same line (`tackleboxZoneUnlocks`) has three live consumers, so this is the weakest shape in the family — **declared-only**, below even write-only, and it slipped through because a reader scanning that line sees a field that is plainly in use immediately beside it. **ak ✅ shipped 2026-08-12** — see the §DOC-02ak row. **al ✅ shipped 2026-08-12** (`lab-report-story-codoex-curse-of-knowedge.md`, 489 → 348 lines — the **third VOICE-not-tokens report**, and the cleanest split: **~26 authored prose specimens, 1 survives (4 %)**, while everything it quoted as existing and marked *keep*
  is still live. **All 3 name failures are its own SECTION HEADINGS contradicted by its own body** — *Voss*/Muffat, *Crane*/Sweelinck, *Finn*/Froberger, all 0 commits ever → **the body wins every time**. - [ ] **§DX-02ax — `drop:false` is a documented protection with five writers, zero readers, and two real items depending on it (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02am).** 🟡 **small design call — enforce or delete, but not both.** `drop:false` occurs **5 times** in `play.html` and is **read 0 times**. The death routine `` `function _storyDeathSaveFall()@26083` `` decides what survives by **item type**, not by the flag: `` `const critTypes = new Set(['shard', 'key']);@26098` `` keeps shards and keys and `` `S_story.inventory = [...critItems, Object.assign({}, STARTER_DAGGER)];@26125` `` sends everything else to the corpse. **For the two tattoos that carry it the flag is harmless** — tattoos live in `S_story.tattoos[]`, never in `S_story.inventory`, so they are safe by *container* and the flag is ornamental. (The lab report's own §III-B asserted the flag "is checked by `_storyDeathSaveFall()`" **in the same sentence** that says the routine "never touches `S_story.tattoos[]`"; the second half is the true one.) **The cost is on the two ordinary inventory items that also carry it and have no `type`:** (1) `` `name:"Innmother's Key"@23557` `` — 0 readers, so losing it costs nothing today (→ §DX-02n (g)); (2) `` `name:"Glut's Gift", icon:'🍯'@31691` `` — a **real gated item**, tested by `` `some(i => i.name === "Glut's Gift")@34711` `` and consumed by `quest_glut_06`, so a death while carrying the jar sends it to the corpse and stalls the return quest until the body is reclaimed. Recoverable, so this is a stumble and not a soft-lock — and **currently unreachable anyway**, because HG1 is non-primary in cell 25,206 (§AUDIT-03x); the moment that row is fixed, this one becomes live. **Only two items in the whole file carry `type:'key'`** (`Cipher Scrap@21296`, `Scholar's Note` in `quest_void_below@21340`), so the protected set is much smaller than the flag's use implies. **The design call is which direction:** (a) teach the death routine to honour the flag — `i.drop === false || critTypes.has(i.type)` — which makes the documented contract true everywhere in one line; or (b) delete `drop:false` from all five sites and give Glut's Gift `type:'key'`, which keeps a single mechanism. **(a) is recommended**: it is smaller, it matches what four documents and the character-creation screen already tell the player, and it makes the flag usable by future authored items without a type taxonomy decision. **Risk:** low either way. **Verify:** die while carrying Glut's Gift → the jar is still in inventory and `quest_glut_06` still completes; shards and keys still survive; the corpse still receives ordinary loot and all gold.

- [ ] **§DX-02az — an NPC carries a dialogue tier no code can ever unlock (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02an).** 🟢 **no design call.** `` `vonn: { meta: { name:"Adjutant Vonn"@10426` `` ships `impartial`, `quote` **and** a `friendly:` array (*"You're one of the few who's actually read the manifest. Most people just shout at the gate."*) — and **nothing in 38,712 lines writes favor for `vonn`**: no `_setNpcFavor('vonn'`, no `{ kind:'favor', npc:'vonn' }`, no `npcFavorability.vonn`. The 2026-05-25 lab report specified him as a **static NPC that does not accumulate favorability**, which the engine still honours; the `friendly` tier was added later (§NPC-01-B's profile expansion), presumably by shape-matching its siblings. **The defect class is new to the family: not a dead FIELD but a dead VALUE inside a live field** — the object is read on every card render, the array is simply never selected. `check:deadconsts` as currently scoped (top-level consts · quest-entry fields · parameters · item objects · `||`-chain alternatives · dead handlers on live entries) cannot see it; the wanted phase is *"a tier keyed by a state the writer set never produces."* Two ways out, both cheap: delete the array, or give Vonn one favor write and let the line pay off — he is the only Conclave officer in the game a player can be *right* about. Related: §DX-02n's roster, §AUDIT-03n's tier machinery.

- [ ] **§DX-02ay — the ten-field Chronicle ledger shape is declared three times, and the factory built to prevent that is called by neither copy (NEW 2026-08-12 during §DOC-02am).** 🟢 **no design call.** `` `const _STAT_ZERO = () => ({ kills:0@23974` `` is the canonical factory and is used correctly at its three runtime sites (`_statTally`'s two null-guards and the respawn reset). But `_S_DEFAULTS()` does **not** call it — it hand-writes the same ten fields twice, at `` `careerStats: { kills:0@23179` `` and on the `runStats:` line immediately below. Three copies of one shape now exist and **all three currently agree** (same ten keys, same order), which is the only reason this is a hazard rather than a live bug: the next field added to the Chronicle has to be added in three places, and a save round-trip will happily carry a two-field-out-of-date ledger forward because `_statTally` back-fills any missing key with `|| 0`. **There is no ordering obstacle** — `` `const _S_DEFAULTS = () => ({@23092` `` is itself an arrow const whose first *call* is `` `S_story = _S_DEFAULTS();@23435` ``, several hundred lines after the factory is defined, so `_STAT_ZERO()` resolves fine at call time. **This is the §STATE-INIT drift class one object deeper**, and the file's own comment at `@22999` — *"seed only … keep `_S_DEFAULTS()` authoritative"* — warns against exactly this pattern one level up. The original lab report specified `careerStats: _STAT_ZERO()` and that is simply what should ship. **Risk:** very low. **Verify:** replace both literals with `_STAT_ZERO()`; a fresh load produces a `S_story` with the identical ten-field ledgers; the §STATE-INIT fresh-load regression test still passes.

- [ ] **§DX-02o — a quest-id string literal in the engine can resolve to nothing, and no gate can see it (found 2026-08-11 during §DOC-02d).** §AUDIT-03j's class — *a missed lookup renders nothing* — in the **quest-id** dimension, which is the one node/npc-style dimension with no fence. **The live instance:** `` `crov:['quest_pit_training','quest_drunk_fight']@23668` `` inside `_hasActiveQuestFor`. There is no `quest_drunk_fight` entry in `QUEST_DB` — `git log -S` returns **1 commit**, the reference itself, so it was never a quest; the drunk pit fight shipped as an engine event (Layer 41's `_drunkFight` branch). `S_story.quests['quest_drunk_fight']` is therefore permanently `undefined`, `_hasActiveQuestFor('crov')` silently drops half its roster, and Weckmann's `questActive` dialogue tier can only ever fire for `quest_pit_training`. Nothing throws. **Corpus measured, and it is small:** **302 distinct `quest_*` literals in the file, 301 resolve, 1 does not** — the only other two misses (`quest_whisper_`, `quest_wane_`) are legitimate `startsWith` prefixes and must be exempted, not fixed. **The gap is structural, not incidental:** `check:questgraph` walks `QUEST_DB` against itself (write-sets, gates, reachability) and never reads engine JS; `check:noderegs` mentions `QUEST_DB` **zero** times. **The durable half is a gate**, the §AUDIT-03j/n/m house style: a `check:questrefs` phase resolving every `'quest_*'` literal outside the `QUEST_DB` section against the live id set, comment-aware (the §AUDIT-03f requirement), with an **explicit** `NOT_A_QUEST_ID` classification list (the two prefixes, plus the `quest_item` type string) so an unlisted miss FAILS. **No design call** — the one live hit is a delete (drop the dangling id from the array) and the rest is the fence. **Risk:** low. **Verify:** the gate exits 1 on a planted bogus id and 0 at HEAD after the delete; `_hasActiveQuestFor('crov')` still returns true while `quest_pit_training` is active.

The anchor pass corrected the four claims it touched, but **did not re-triage the seeds** — that is this row. Affected: **§POT-C5** (moral ledger — *"ships after §VM-01 Inc A, not before"*), **§POT-H4** (factions respond), **§POT-B4** (skill-check chains), **§POT-R1** (⭐ the keystone, still marked `BLOCKED = §VM-01 Inc A`), **§POT-R3**, **§POT-S2**, and **§POT-X3** (blocked on §VM-01-F, which also shipped). The BACKLOG §POT-PROMOTE preamble repeats the same stale gating. **The honest caveat, and why this is a real row and not a find-replace:** `choice` has **0 authors**, so nothing has yet proven the opcode end-to-end through authored content — the first consumer should be a small exemplar, not seven arcs at once. **Do first:** author one `choice` quest and verify it through `./api.sh advise` + the driver, then re-triage the seven seeds against what that proves. **Needs a design call** on which seed goes first (§POT-R1 is the file's own ⭐, §POT-B4 the cheapest). **Risk:** low to re-triage (doc-only); the exemplar is real work and may want a lab report if it grows past one quest.

### §DOC-02 — Lab-report verification program (NEW 2026-08-11)

> **Premise (user directive 2026-08-11):** the **107** files in `docs/lab-reports/` are the project's design record, and most were written *before* the thing they describe shipped. They are HISTORY docs (`src/scripts/legacy-codes.js`) — never rewritten to hide what was believed at the time — but a spec that was never checked against the code reads exactly like a spec that was. The program: **take the oldest report by mtime, re-measure every claim against `play.html`, rewrite it short and IEEE-shaped** (abstract · method · as-built inventory with `symbol@line` anchors · spec→shipped delta table · risk-register outcome · defects → BACKLOG rows), and file everything it finds. **The house rules that govern this work:** claims that did not ship are marked **NOT SHIPPED** and *kept*, never deleted (a silently removed claim reads as one that held); never read a node code off a doc table (§AUDIT-03m); and annotation without verification launders a wrong claim into a confident-looking live one (§AUDIT-03m-FU). **Ordering note (corrected 2026-08-11 by §DOC-02b — re-measured, the §DOC-02a figures were wrong):** the directory holds **107** `.md` files, not 109 (`git ls-files docs/lab-reports/ | wc -l`; six were deleted by `120d617`). **44** of them shared one mtime (`2026-06-09 11:06:54`, a bulk copy) when the program opened — so "oldest" within that block is resolved alphabetically — and there are **45 distinct mtimes** in all. Each verified report gets a fresh mtime and leaves the block, so the block shrinks by one per increment (43 after §DOC-02a, 42 after §DOC-02b); **re-derive the ordering from `stat`, never from this note's arithmetic.**

> *Closed rows §DOC-02a · §DOC-02b · §DOC-02c · §DOC-02d · §DOC-02e · §DOC-02f · §DOC-02g · §DOC-02h · §DOC-02i · §DOC-02j · §DOC-02k · §DOC-02l · §DOC-02m · §DOC-02n · §DOC-02o · §DOC-02p · §DOC-02q · §DOC-02r · §DOC-02s · §DOC-02z · §DOC-02aj · §DOC-02ak · §DOC-02al moved verbatim to [plan-archive.md](plan-archive.md) §"Archived 2026-10-07 — closed-row sweep".*

- [ ] **§DOC-02v… — the remaining 69 reports.** Next by the ordering rule (re-derive from `stat`, see the preamble): re-derive from `stat` (**7 files left in the 2026-06-09 11:06:54 block** after §DOC-02al), … through the 2026-06-09 block, then the distinct-mtime files oldest-first. **A sixth accelerator, added by §DOC-02f and mandatory for any claim about the past: read the ARCHIVE, not HEAD.** `git show <earliest-commit>:play.html` is the only instrument that can say what the file actually contained when the report was written; HEAD can only say what it contains now, and the difference is where two of this program's corrections have come from. Pair it with `git log -S` (accelerator four): **zero commits proves non-existence; absence at HEAD proves nothing.** **A seventh, added by the same pass: weight a report's tables and function bodies as evidence, and its traces, walkthroughs and sequence diagrams as claims.** §DOC-02f's data listings had a zero error rate while its execution
  traces carried five deltas including three identifiers with no commits ever — in one file, by one author, on one day. The predictor is not whether a passage cites code but whether the author could **copy** it. **An eighth, and it is a gate hazard rather than a method: an anchor whose text is not a LITERAL substring of the line it points at is DEAD even when the line is perfectly alive.** §DOC-02f found **5 such anchors left by §DOC-02e** plus 1 of its own, and none of the six symbols had moved — all six lines existed, unchanged, at the cached number. The three ways to mint one, written here **without their `@line` suffixes so this row does not itself mint three more** — a counterexample inside backticks *is* an anchor to the gate, which is how §DOC-02f briefly re-broke it: **markdown table pipe-escaping** (an anchor reading `q.retryGateDays \|\| 1`, where `\|` is the table escape and the source says `||`), an **ellipsis** standing in for elided code (`checkLevelUp: () => { … _checkLevelUp(); }`),
  and **whitespace normalisation** (an anchor reading `const d20 = …` where the source has `const d20  =` with two spaces). The fix is always to shorten the anchor to the shortest unambiguous *verbatim* fragment — `` `q.retryGateDays@6822` ``, `` `checkLevelUp: () =>@22376` `` — never to widen it into a pretty quotation. **Related and worth stating plainly: §DOC-02e recorded `check:walk` 16/16 green while carrying those 5 dead anchors**, because the run hit the known harness detach artifact and never reached gate 15. *When the detach artifact fires, the gates that did not run are not green — re-run them individually before recording a result.* **Scope one report per increment** — §DOC-02a produced 2 audit rows + 2 doc corrections, §DOC-02b 1 defect row + 1 ordering correction, and §DOC-02c 2 defect rows, all from a single report each. **A fourth accelerator, added by §DOC-02c and now mandatory for any report with dead symbols:** run `git log -S "<symbol>" -- play.html` on every
  symbol the census marks dead. It costs one command per symbol and separates **RETIRED** (shipped, later removed — the report was true when written) from **NOT SHIPPED** (never existed under that name — the report was written from intent, not from the file). §DOC-02c's headline finding came from exactly this: `stalkModal` and two of its DOM ids return **0 commits ever**, while the feature they describe shipped three days later under different names. *A report cannot make this distinction about itself; only history can.* **A fifth accelerator, added by §DOC-02d:** when a report names a character two ways, `grep` **both** names in the engine before writing the delta table — the split is usually still there as two keys, and it is the highest-value finding a content report can yield (§AUDIT-03n cost a whole row to repair one). **Three accelerators, now proven three times:** (1) the arc's *maintained* home doc (`story.md`/`world.md`) is usually already correct and annotated, so the lab
  report's job is the **delta table**, not a second copy of it; (2) the fastest first measurement is always `grep -c` on every key the report claims to have added — a missing entry is a delta, a present entry still needs its *fields* compared; and (3) **for any report that enumerates symbols, batch the whole name list through one `grep -c` loop before reading a line of it** — §DOC-02b's 197-symbol census took one command and immediately partitioned the report into 180 live claims and 17 dead subsystems, which is the shape of the delta table. **One thing to watch for that §DOC-02b found and §DOC-02a did not:** a long report can **contradict itself**, and the tiebreak is mechanical — *believe the section that cites a line number.*
